    Compatibility¶

    This client aims for full API parity with the reference PySpark Spark Connect client (pyspark.sql.connect.*) and the shared modules it depends on. The goal is that existing Spark Connect code runs unchanged and gets byte-for-byte identical results. For how this client relates to the pyspark and pyspark-client packages on PyPI - and how to tell which one you have installed - see Which client am I using?.

    Supported Spark¶

    Apache Spark 4.3.0 and later. A release of the crates and the wheel has the version of the Spark release it targets (starting at 4.3.0), so the version number of a release tells you which Spark it speaks. The master branch carries the next development version (4.4.0 now), and speaks the Spark release that it pins (4.3.0) until it moves to the next one.

    Rust migration for the 4.3 line¶

    These are the changes for Rust users from v4.2.0 to v4.3.0.

    Value and LiteralExpression add a DayTimeInterval(i64) variant for signed microseconds. Downstream exhaustive matches on these public enums must handle the new variant. A collected map keeps its keys typed: Value::Map is a Vec<(Value, Value)> of its entries in order, where it was a BTreeMap<String, Value> of the text of its keys, so a day-time interval key is a Value::DayTimeInterval (a timedelta in Python, as in PySpark), and create_dataframe takes such keys. Value::Struct holds a StructValue (Row::new(names, values).into()), where it held a Vec<(String, Value)>. Value adds TimestampNtz(i64), the microseconds of a TIMESTAMP_NTZ value, which collected as a Value::Timestamp before, and Time(i64), the nanoseconds since midnight of a TIME value, which collected as its ISO text in a Value::String. DataType adds TimestampNtzNanos and TimestampLtzNanos; SparkErrorKind adds BuiltinOverflowError, BuiltinRuntimeError, BuiltinTypeError, BuiltinValueError, ConnectMessage, RecursionError, Spark, UnicodeDecode and UnicodeEncode; and LogicalPlan adds ApplyInPandasWithState, AsOfJoin, CachedRelation, ChunkedCachedLocalRelation, HtmlString, Parse, Raw, ShowString, Unbuildable and WithRelations. None of these enums is #[non_exhaustive], so an exhaustive match of one of them must handle its new variants.

    DataFrame::sort, order_by, sort_within_partitions, agg, observe, observe_with, hint_expressions, repartition_by_range, repartition_by_range_opt, repartition_by_expressions, GroupedData::agg, the partition_by and order_by of Window and WindowSpec, and TableValuedFunction::udtf take impl IntoIterator<Item = impl Into<Column>>, as select and group_by do: Columns, column names or expressions, such as df.sort([col("x").desc()]) and .agg([sum(col("id")).alias("total")]), without .expression().clone(). So does the first list of drop_columns, of column references: a name in it is col(name), as in PySpark's drop(F.col(name)), so a name of several columns is AMBIGUOUS_REFERENCE, and a dotted name such as a.b refers to the field b of a column a, which drops nothing ("`a.b`" refers to a column named a.b). Its second list is of plain names, as that of drop: a name drops every column of that name, and a.b the column named a.b. grouping_sets takes any iterable of grouping sets, each any iterable of Columns, such as [[col("a")], [col("a"), col("b")]], and grouping_sets_with_columns takes such sets and any iterable of the grouping Columns. The names of drop, describe, to_df, to_schema, melt, the numeric aggregates of GroupedData (sum, avg, min, max, mean, numeric_agg), the freq_items and approx_quantile of StatFunctions, Column::drop_fields and the partition_by and cluster_by of DataStreamWriter take any iterable of names, as do the SQL expressions of select_expr, the statistics of summary and the paths of DataFrameReader::load_paths. The (name, Column) pairs of with_columns and the (old, new) pairs of with_columns_renamed take any iterable of pairs, and so do the (String, Value) pairs of fillna_map, such as a BTreeMap<String, Value> (its names stay Strings, so that vec![] and ("id".into(), value) still infer their type). functions::count_distinct_columns and UserDefinedFunction::to_expression (wasm) take any Columns. A Vec or an array of them compiles as before. The Vec<Expression> that the sort and aggregate methods took still compiles, by the new From<Expression> for Column, and so does the Vec<SortOrder> of the order_by of a window, by From<SortOrder> for Column; there a Column that is not a sort order is sorted ascending, nulls first, as PySpark's WindowSpec.orderBy sorts it. An empty vec![] or [] has no item type to infer: pass Vec::<Column>::new() or Vec::<&str>::new() instead. This breaks a caller of v4.2.0 that passes an empty literal to select_expr or summary, which took a Vec<&str> there: df.summary(vec![]), for the default statistics, becomes df.summary(Vec::<&str>::new()). load_paths is new in this release: v4.2.0 had only load, so no v4.2.0 caller breaks on it, and a new caller passes an empty literal as load_paths(Vec::<&str>::new()). The optional subset arguments of the null functions, the optional value_vars of melt and the optional column_names of drop_duplicates and drop_duplicates_within_watermark stay Option<Vec<&str>>, as in v4.2.0, so that None still infers its type. The optional values of unpivot takes any iterable, as in v4.2.0, so its None has no item type to infer, unlike that of melt: pass None::<Vec<Column>> or None::<[&str; 0]> to unpivot every column that is not an id.

    BinaryClassificationEvaluator::set_score_col and score_col, and the Python scoreCol= argument and setScoreCol of v4.2.0 are removed, without an alias: Spark's evaluator has no scoreCol param, so no value of it worked on the server. Use set_raw_prediction_col and raw_prediction_col in Rust, and rawPredictionCol= / setRawPredictionCol in Python. They follow standard pyspark.ml.evaluation.BinaryClassificationEvaluator, whose default input column is rawPrediction and whose scores may be doubles or vectors.

    The native ML classes take a JVM Int param as an i32: LogisticRegression::set_max_iter, LinearSVC::set_max_iter and LinearSVC::set_aggregation_depth take an i32, and LogisticRegression::max_iter returns one. Every native estimator has a getter of each of its params (KMeans::k, RandomForestClassifier::num_trees, ...), which reads its Params: the value that is set, else Spark's default, which for seed is the Java hash code of the JVM class name, the seed that the server takes when none is sent (PySpark's getSeed gives Python's hash of the class name, which it does not send), and None for weight_col and thresholds, which have none. The fields of LogisticRegressionModel, StringIndexerModel, MaxAbsScalerModel and PipelineModel are private, as those of the other fitted models: use Transformer::operator and Transformer::params, LogisticRegressionModel::features_col, label_col and prediction_col, and PipelineModel::stages. VectorAssembler::input_cols returns a Vec<String>, and VectorAssembler::set_input_cols takes impl IntoIterator<Item = impl Into<String>>. Pipeline::set_stages takes the stages themselves, impl IntoIterator<Item = PipelineStage>, where it took their names, and Pipeline::stages returns them, a &[PipelineStage]. The input_col of StandardScaler, MaxAbsScaler and StringIndexer returns an Option<&str>, which is None unless set: as in Spark, it has no default. Their output_col, and that of VectorAssembler, return a String, Spark's <uid>__output unless set, the column that the server writes. Before, they gave names that the server never wrote, such as scaled_features.

    Params::set_param_int32, set_param_int_narrow, set_param_string_array and set_param_double_array are public, with the new Params::clear_param, and the evaluators have a params_mut, as estimators and transformers do. Seeds, CrossValidator::set_seed's included, are sent as the narrowest integer literal that holds them, as PySpark sends a Python int. MlOperator::new makes a uid of the simple class name and 12 hex digits (KMeans_1a2b3c4d5e6f), as PySpark and Spark do, instead of a bare UUID.

    DataFrame::fillna_value and DataFrame::fillna_map return a Result, as dropna does. As PySpark's fillna, they take a bool, an integer, a float or a string (an integer is sent as a long, a float as a double), and reject other values with NOT_EXPECTED_TYPE and an empty map with CANNOT_BE_EMPTY. Before, such a value filled string columns with its debug text. A Value::Map given to fillna_value fills the columns of its keys, as PySpark's fillna of a dict, instead of being rejected. fillna, fillna_double, fillna_string and fillna_bool are unchanged.

    A transport failure has the gRPC code that grpc-python gives it, which PySpark's errors and retries see. SparkError::grpc_code is UNAVAILABLE (14) for a connection that closes or is reset under a call, where it was UNKNOWN (2); for a call that its connection closes before sending it, where it was CANCELLED (1); and for a connection that the server ends with a GOAWAY, where it was the code of the reason of the GOAWAY, such as INTERNAL (13) for NO_ERROR and RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED (8) for ENHANCE_YOUR_CALM. It is INTERNAL (13) for a stream that the server resets with a reason that tonic leaves unmapped, such as STREAM_CLOSED, which is no longer retried.

    The 4.3 line changes the public Rust representation of expressions and logical plans to share immutable nodes. This is a breaking change for code that directly constructs or matches these representations. The Column and DataFrame APIs, Python APIs, and Spark Connect protocol are unchanged by this representation change.

    Expression is now a shared handle; its previous enum variants live in ExpressionNode. Construct an ExpressionNode and convert it with .into() (or pass it to Expression::new). Match on expression.node(), which borrows an &ExpressionNode. Cloning an Expression shares its immutable node instead of recursively copying its children. To change an expression, construct a new node. Common nodes also have Expression::literal, Expression::column, and Expression::function constructors.

    For example, replace direct Expression::Literal(...) construction and matching with:

    use spark_connect::expression::{Expression, ExpressionNode, LiteralExpression};

let expression: Expression = ExpressionNode::Literal(LiteralExpression::Long(1)).into();
let shared = expression.clone();
match shared.node() {
    ExpressionNode::Literal(LiteralExpression::Long(value)) => assert_eq!(*value, 1),
    _ => panic!("expected a long literal"),
}

    Child expressions are handles too: ExpressionNode::DirectShufflePartitionId takes an Expression, and CaseWhen::else_expr is Option<Expression>, without the previous extra Box.

    Logical-plan child fields now use Arc<LogicalPlan> instead of Box<LogicalPlan>. When constructing variants directly, replace Box::new(child) with Arc::new(child), or clone an existing Arc to share that child. Plan-building helpers accept impl Into<Arc<LogicalPlan>>, so they accept either an owned LogicalPlan or an existing Arc<LogicalPlan>. Serialization still produces the same protobuf tree; sharing does not deduplicate repeated relations on the wire.

    LogicalPlan implements Drop, so that a plan of many levels is dropped without recursing. A field can therefore not be moved out of an owned LogicalPlan: destructuring one by value is a compile error (E0509). Match on a reference instead (match &plan, or match plan.as_ref() of an Arc<LogicalPlan>) and clone the fields you need; the inputs are Arcs, so cloning them shares them.

    The new LogicalPlan::Raw { relation, owner, depth, observations } is a relation built outside the core, as pyspark.ml builds one over its input. Its depth is the levels of its relation as a plan, with those of the DataFrames that it holds, which count for MAX_PLAN_DEPTH, as PySpark's serializer counts the plan of the input within its own. Code that constructs the variant directly sets it to 1 for a relation without nested plans. MAX_PLAN_DEPTH (746) guards the stack of this client, and is not PySpark's limit: PySpark's serializer, with upb, sends a plan of up to about 990 levels, which the server fails from about 510 levels. LogicalPlan::try_to_proto serializes a plan as a request does: a plan deeper than MAX_PLAN_DEPTH, or with an expression deeper than MAX_EXPRESSION_DEPTH, is a RecursionError. to_proto still serializes a plan of any depth, but prost encodes and drops a protobuf by recursing, so the proto of a plan of many thousands of levels can overflow a small stack.

    Value and LiteralExpression add a YearMonthInterval(i32) variant for signed months. Collect gives it for a year-month interval, where it gave the text of the interval (Value::String("1-0")), so that a pivot on collected values names its columns as the pivot without values does (INTERVAL '1-0' YEAR TO MONTH), and create_dataframe takes it for an INTERVAL YEAR TO MONTH column (not for INTERVAL YEAR or INTERVAL MONTH, which the server cannot narrow the Arrow interval to). Downstream exhaustive matches on these enums must handle the new variant. The Python client still raises NOT_IMPLEMENTED when it collects a year-month interval, as PySpark does.

    DataFrame::repartition_by_range and repartition_by_range_opt return a Result. As PySpark's repartitionByRange, a partition count that is not positive is VALUE_NOT_POSITIVE and an empty column list CANNOT_BE_EMPTY. Before, the server split the rows round-robin for an empty column list. StatFunctions::sample_by returns a Result too: a stratum that is not a literal of a bool, an integer, a float or a string (a null, a decimal, binary, a date, a timestamp, an interval or an array) is DISALLOWED_TYPE_FOR_CONTAINER, as PySpark rejects a key that is not a float, an int or a str. Before, it was sent as an empty literal, which failed only when the DataFrame ran, or as its own literal, which sampled no rows. Without a seed, sample_by sends one that the client draws, as sample and random_split do.

    A panic of a native StreamingQueryListener on an event of the listener bus is isolated, as on the started event and as PySpark isolates the exception of a listener: it is reported, the other listeners still get the event, and the subscription goes on. Before, it ended the subscription and removed every listener. An error that try_on_event returns still ends it.

    The random functions rand, randn, uuid, shuffle, uniform, randstr and count_min_sketch draw a new seed for each call, as PySpark and the Scala client do. Before, each sent the same fixed seed, so two uuid() columns, or the uuid() of two runs, were equal. For reproducible values, use the _with_seed variants (rand_with_seed(42), uuid_with_seed(lit(42)), ...). random and random_with_seed are added as the aliases of rand that PySpark has.

    DataFrameWriter::clear_bucket_by is added: it unsets the bucketing of a writer, so that its write sends none, as PySpark's bucketBy of a count that is not positive, the last bucketBy winning. bucket_by is unchanged and sends the count it is given.

    DataFrame::persist takes impl Into<Option<StorageLevel>>, so a StorageLevel is passed as before. None sends no storage level, as PySpark's persist(None), and the server then caches at its spark.sql.defaultCacheStorageLevel. cache is unchanged and persists at MEMORY_AND_DISK_DESER.

    DataType::from_json reads the spatial types as the JSON reader of Spark does: a bare geometry or geography is of SRID 4326, geography(<algorithm>) is of the CRS OGC:CRS84, a CRS is of the form <authority>:<code>, and an algorithm other than SPHERICAL is ST_INVALID_ALGORITHM_VALUE. It no longer takes geometry(<srid>), geometry(any) and their geography forms, which are of the DDL, not of the JSON, of Spark. DataType::from_ddl takes a number or any as an SRID, as the DDL parser of Spark does, and no longer takes such forms as geometry(+5), geometry(SRID:ANY) or geography(4326, SPHERICAL).

    Rust migration for the 4.4 line (unreleased)¶

    These are the changes for Rust users after v4.3.0, on master, which ship in 4.4.0.

    SparkSessionBuilder adds config, configs, app_name, master and create, as PySpark's SparkSession.builder of Spark Connect: the session gets the options as configurations once it connects, in one silent ConfigRequest, in the order that they were first given and of their last values, but spark.remote and spark.master; app_name sets spark.app.name, master sets nothing, and remote(url) is config("spark.remote", url). The Rust API keeps no active or default session, so get_or_create creates a session at each call, as create does, where PySpark's getOrCreate returns its active or default session.

    SparkErrorKind adds BuiltinAttributeError and BuiltinAssertionError, Python's AttributeError and AssertionError as PySpark's client raises them, which SparkError::builtin_attribute and SparkError::builtin_assertion make. SparkErrorKind is not #[non_exhaustive], so an exhaustive match of it must handle them. ServerErrorInfo adds a hidden rpc_error, grpcio's error of the call that failed (an error::GrpcioError), metadata, the metadata of its ErrorInfo, and fetched_parameters, whether FetchErrorDetails gave its message parameters, which are not a stable API: a struct expression of ServerErrorInfo that names every field adds them, or takes the others of ..ServerErrorInfo::default().

    LiteralExpression adds a hidden ByteArray { value, rendered } variant, the literal of a Python bytearray, which the Python client makes: it is sent as a Binary literal of value, and its render is rendered, bytearray(b'...'), as PySpark renders a bytearray. It is not a stable API; downstream exhaustive matches on LiteralExpression must handle it, or have a wildcard arm.

    ExpressionNode adds a hidden PlanBuildOrigin variant, an expression that the Python client makes when it builds a plan, such as the & of the conditions of a join of a list of Columns, so that its origin is of that time, as PySpark makes it then. CastTarget adds a hidden Shown(DataType, String) variant, a cast that sends its type as CastTarget::Type does and renders as the given string, the simpleString() of the Python type object, as PySpark's CastExpression.__repr__ renders it, and a hidden Read(Box<CastRead>) variant, of Column::cast_read, a cast of a type object of the Python client that reads it when it renders and when a request builds the plan, as PySpark's CastExpression does: the readers of the CastRead give the text that the render shows and the spark.connect.DataType that the request sends, of the errors of each then. None is a stable API; downstream exhaustive matches on ExpressionNode and CastTarget must handle them, or have a wildcard arm.

    GroupedData::transform_with_state takes the output schema of its TransformWithStateInfo, as transform_with_state_in_pandas does, where it sent the return type of its function: PySpark's transformWithState sends outputStructType as it is given, a DDL string as an unparsed type, which the server parses. The update and insert of the clauses of MergeIntoWriter take impl IntoIterator<Item = (K, Column)> of K: Into<String>, so a HashMap<String, Column> is passed as before, and the merge sends the assignments in their order, as PySpark sends those of assignments.items(): a Vec or a BTreeMap keeps an order, and a HashMap sends its own.

    Params::sendable serializes the params as a fit, a transform, an evaluation and a save send them, as PySpark's serialize_ml_params: of the error of a value that PySpark cannot send, before any request. Params::extend_from sets the params of another Params over them, as a copy of PySpark of a param map sets its values. Params::set_param_int_array sends a list of ints as PySpark's literal of it, of the type of its first element, where it sent a long array when an element did not fit 32 bits. PredictionModel::num_features and VectorIndexerModel::category_maps of an int out of the range of an i32 are an error, where they truncated it, and a grid int beyond a long, such as a whole float 1e20 of an int param, is PySpark's VALUE_NOT_BETWEEN, where it saturated to i64::MAX. OneVsRest::max_label returns the Value of the largest label, of which the fit takes Python's int(), where it returned an Option<f64>; OneVsRest::fit_classes takes the weight column of the fit, and returns the binary training set of each class with the model; OneVsRest::check_required_params is removed, as the fit raises PySpark's errors in PySpark's order; and OneVsRestModel adds new and set_classifier, and its apply_param_map applies the map to each of its models too, as PySpark's OneVsRestModel.copy copies them (see Native OneVsRest). Model::fit_warning and MlRef::fit_warning give the warning that the server sends with the model of a fit, and SparkSession::set_ml_fit_warning_handler sets the handler that each fit calls with it (see Native ML objects). MlRef::of_reference (not a stable API) is an object of the ML cache that the client does not own, whose last owner runs a release of its owner in place of its deletion, MlRef::keep keeps an object on the server at the drop of its last owner, MlRef::is_unshared tells an owner of no clone, MlRef::release deletes an object while its owners live, once, MlRef::owners, MlRef::is and MlRef::is_owned count its owners, tell a clone of one and an object of the client, and Model::training_summary gives the target of the training summary that a model holds (not stable APIs either), and PickleReach, pickle_reach_with and expression_pickle_reach_with of spark_connect::ml (not a stable API) give what PySpark's pickle of a DataFrame or of an expression reaches of the objects of pyspark.ml that its plans keep, and the functions of its UDFs before them, in PySpark's order, of the reach that a callback gives of the owner of a relation built outside the core.

    RetryPolicy adds a retries_failures field, true by default: a policy of false retries no failure, as PySpark's own RetryPolicy, whose can_retry takes no failure, retries none, and RetryPolicy::no_retries is such a policy. The jitter of each retry is a draw of its own, uniform over its range, as PySpark's random.uniform(0, jitter) draws it, where the retries of calls that failed together waited the same jitter. A struct expression of RetryPolicy that names every field adds it, or takes the others of ..RetryPolicy::default(). The core tells the listener of retries::set_listener (a RetriesListener, not a stable API) when the retries of a failure that a policy retries are spent, where PySpark's Retrying warns [RETRIES_EXCEEDED], and its error is the error of the call; when a request that it sends in the background, which no caller waits for, fails (background_failure, of a retries::BackgroundRequest and a retries::BackgroundFailure), where PySpark's sender warns and logs; and of what PySpark's client logs of its retries and of its errors (retrying, retrying_at_once, given_up, server_retry_delay, rpc_error and error_info), and, in the dialect of grpcio, of a wait before a retry that is negative or of decades, which the Python binding sleeps of Python's time.sleep (sleep_long, of a retries::LongSleep, which the call awaits). These methods have default bodies that do nothing. The listener is one of the process, for the calls of every client: the Python binding sets it, to warn from the thread of the call and to log to the logger of Spark Connect. SparkError adds a hidden pyspark_message, the message of PySpark's exception of a gRPC failure whose status carries no error of the server, which the Python binding raises: the text of grpcio's error of the call, as PySpark's _handle_rpc_error raises it; and a hidden grpcio, grpcio's error of a failed call that PySpark's client leaves to grpc, which the Python binding raises as grpcio's RpcError (an error::GrpcioError of error::grpcio_error, not a stable API), set only in the dialect of grpcio (see below). The message of the error of the Rust API is still the description of the code in brackets and the message of the status. A struct expression of SparkError that names every field adds them, or takes the others of an error of its constructors, such as ..SparkError::connect_msg(message). SparkError::from_grpc_status_of converts the status of a call of a stream of responses (GrpcCall::ServerStreaming, of grpcio's _MultiThreadedRendezvous). The retries of the calls of a client are decided by its RetryPolicy, or by a retries::RetryDecider that SparkConnectClient::set_retry_decider and SparkSession::set_retry_decider set in its place, of a retries::CallRetries for each call that gives a retries::Decision of each failed attempt: as the Python binding runs PySpark's Retrying of policies that the core does not retry with. These are not a stable API. The reads of the configs of the server's stack trace, as the error of the server of a call is converted, are retried under the retry policy of the client, as PySpark's config() retries them, where each was read once.

    The module client_log of the core (not a stable API) has what PySpark's client logs of its calls: the listener of the process (client_log::set_listener, a client_log::Listener, which the Python binding sets) is asked whether a level is logged and given each record of one, a client_log::Message of a text, or of an encoded message of protobuf with the text before it, which the binding writes of PySpark's _proto_to_string, of a retries::Delivery whose error, as of a handler of the logger that raised, fails the call at the record (client_log::log is #[must_use]: a record is written as its delivery is awaited), and which writes the records of a thread that client_log::write_records_on_this_thread marks on that thread, as those of the listener bus of streaming queries. SparkConnectClient::execute_plan_reattachable logs as PySpark's to_table a plan of a relation, and as its execute_command a command; SparkConnectClient::execute_plan_reattachable_logged logs as the method of a client_log::ExecutionLog, of the plan compressed, and SparkConnectClient::execute_plan_reattachable_as_is of the plan as it is, as the plan that SparkConnectClient::prepare_plan prepared, of one try of its compression, as PySpark's to_proto tries it; SparkConnectClient::build_execution and SparkConnectClient::execute_built make the two parts of such an execution, before PySpark's Progress of it starts and in it (a client::BuiltExecution); SparkConnectClient::analyze_plan_logged logs as the method of a client_log::AnalysisLog, of the plans compressed, and SparkConnectClient::analyze_plan_as_is of the plans as they are; client_log::explain_plan is the record of PySpark's explain_string, of the mode as it is given; and SparkSession::set_thread_call_stack and SparkConnectClient::set_thread_call_stack give the requests of a thread the call stack of the user's code of their call. SparkSession::execute_plan_proto_logged, SparkSession::resolved_plan_proto, SparkSession::compressed_plan_proto, SparkSession::known_plan_compression (of SparkConnectClient::known_plan_compression, the threshold of the plan compression once its settings are read) and SparkSession::result_chunking serve the plans and the requests of the Python client, and client::converting_failure gives the failure that the calling thread converts, of grpcio's error and of the operation ID of its execution (a client::Conversion), during which a signal interrupts nothing, as during PySpark's _handle_error, and SparkConnectClient::inside_error_handling (and SparkSession::inside_error_handling) whether the calling thread converts a failure of the client, during which the failure of another of its calls is not converted, as PySpark's inside_error_handling tells it: of a call that runs within a poll of the conversion, as of a handler of one of its records or of a RetriesListener, also through the blocking API, or on the thread of a call of the Python binding whose conversion runs, as of a signal handler, but not of another task of an async caller of the client, which the thread polls between two polls of the conversion. The methods of a RetriesListener of what PySpark's client logs of its retries (retrying, retrying_at_once, given_up, server_retry_delay) return the error of the record, which fails the call, and RetriesListener::raised_in_handling is given the error of a record that the client writes as it handles the failure of a call, of that failure (a retries::Handled: the failed RPC of a conversion, or the error of the failed lookup of the plan compression), and gives the error of the call. All of these are hidden. Without a listener, nothing is logged. A closed client, as of a stopped session, writes the records of a call before it fails it with NO_ACTIVE_SESSION, where it failed it first, and the first plan of a closed client looks the plan compression up, which fails of the closed client and disables the compression, as PySpark's. A plan is compressed in a frame of zstd of the size of its content, as python-zstandard's compress, where it was a frame of no size, so the server's PLAN_SIZE_LARGER_THAN_MAX of a plan over its limit reports the size that the frame declares, as of PySpark's plans, and the threshold of the compression is read as Python's int() reads it (+1_000).

    SparkConnectClient::read_plan_compression reads the plan compression settings of the server before a plan is sent, as PySpark's client reads them when it builds its first plan: before the request of a relation (to_proto), which then observes the server-side session ID of the response of the read, and after the request of a command (execute_command), so the first command of a session observes none. SparkConnectClient::execute_plan_reattachable_uncompressed sends a plan as it is, neither compressed nor after that read, as PySpark's registrations of functions, table functions, data sources and Java functions set their command in the plan of their request, which the registrations of the core do.

    SparkSession adds the settings of PySpark's client: retry_policy and set_retry_policy, as PySpark's get_retry_policies and set_retry_policies; reattachable_execute and set_reattachable_execute, as its enable_reattachable_execute and disable_reattachable_execute; token_parameter, the token parameter of the connection string; rpc_deadlines, the RpcDeadlines of its calls; and set_global_user_context_extensions, which replaces the global user context extensions, as a change of PySpark's global_user_context_extensions. The clones of a client share its retry policy, where each clone had a policy of its own, and the client of a new session, of SparkConnectClient::with_new_session_id and clone_session (and so of SparkSession::new_session and clone_session), has the default policy and chunking and no decider of its retries, as PySpark's newSession and clone, where it kept the policy of the client. An execution that is not reattachable, as ExecutePlanResponseReattachableIterator::not_reattachable makes its iterator, is one ExecutePlan, sent again from the start when its stream fails in a way that the retry policy retries, and never released, as PySpark's.     RpcDeadlines adds a get_status field, the deadline of the GetStatus RPC, of 10 minutes by default and none in RpcDeadlines::disabled(), as PySpark's RpcDeadlines.get_status: a struct expression of RpcDeadlines that names every field adds it, or takes the others of ..RpcDeadlines::default(). SparkConnectClient::get_status_proto sends the GetStatus RPC of a request, of the session ID, the user, the client type and the observed server-side session ID of the client, under its retry policy and the get_status deadline, as PySpark's _get_operation_statuses. ChannelBuilder::params gives the parameters of the connection string, in the order of their keys, and ChannelBuilder::remove removes one, as PySpark's newSession pops the session ID of the channel builder of its client.

    A channel connects to the addresses of its host, or of its proxy, as grpcio's: the next one 250 ms after the previous one or once the previous one failed, each with a connect deadline of 20 s of its own, which its TCP connection, the tunnel of a proxy, its TLS handshake and the HTTP/2 settings that the server sends first share, of a TLS connection too, and which bounds the lookup of the name too. A connection is the process's, of one address, as a subchannel of gRPC's global subchannel pool: the clients whose channels resolve the target to that address, of the same proxy and its credentials, TLS and keepalive, and of no token, share it, as gRPC's pool shares the subchannels of the channels of a process. Each client has a channel of its own, which resolves the name of the target as each call that needs a connection connects, and takes a connection of its addresses that another client made, the first of them in their order, else connects to them in turn: the clients of SparkConnectClient::with_new_session_id and clone_session share the connection of their client, as before, and so does a client of another connect of the same target, which had a connection of its own, while their names resolve to its address; a client whose name resolves to other addresses connects to them, where it took the connection of the target that the process had. A client of a connection string of a token has a connection of its own, also of a new session or a clone of it, and of another connect of the same token, as grpcio's channels of the credentials of a token share no connection, where its new sessions shared the connection of the client; the clones of a client (Clone) share its channel. A connection ends once no open client holds it: SparkConnectClient::close lets go of it, as grpcio's close of a channel, where it stayed while the closed client lived. The window of a connection is the largest of HTTP/2, so that the results that a client does not read on, as of executions that it holds open, do not hold the calls of the other clients of the connection, nor its other calls, where a connection of five such results of 2 MiB waited, as gRPC's connections, whose windows grow with those of their streams, do not wait; the window of each stream, of 2 MiB, bounds what it holds unread. The channels run on the shared runtime of the core (runtime::get_runtime), not on the runtime of the caller, as they are the process's: a caller's runtime, or its paused clock of a test, does not drive their timers. A child of a fork() gets a shared runtime and channels of its own, as a parent's runtime has no thread there: the clients of the parent take a channel of the child's in the child, as grpcio's fork support resets the channels of a parent, and runtime::fork_generation (not a stable API) tells the generation of the process, of which a handler of pthread_atfork makes another in each child. The child leaves the objects of its parent's runtime as they are, never dropped there: a client of the parent that the child drops leaves the connection of its channel to the parent, where the drop removed the socket of a connection that the channel held from the epoll instance that the child shares with its parent, so that the parent's next call on it waited forever, and a stream of an execution of the parent that the child drops is not released, as the parent reads it. As the fork returns, the child closes the sockets of the connections of its parent and the epoll instances of its parent's runtime, without a call on the epoll instance, so that a connection of the parent ends on the server with the parent, as grpcio's fork support closes the descriptors of the event engine of a parent, and makes the eventfd of the runtime of each of its parents an eventfd of its own, rather than closing it: an object of the parent that wakes a task of that runtime writes to it, which a descriptor of the child could take the number of. A child then holds one descriptor of each generation before it, where it held the three of the runtime of each of its parents, and the sockets of their connections. The state that the threads of the process keep under a lock is of each generation, or read without one, so that a lock that a thread of the parent held at the fork does not hold the child: the runtime, the channels, the limit of the logs of the failed tunnels of a proxy, the jitter of the retries, which a child draws of a seed of its own, as Python seeds random again in a child, and, of a client, its session IDs and its retry policy, which a child keeps, and its executions in flight, of which a child has none of its parent's. A fork while another thread makes a call can leave the child waiting, as grpcio's fork support asks that no other thread makes a call then, and so can a fork while the client makes a call in the background (a release of an execution, the removal of a cached relation or of a model, the reads of to_local_iterator of prefetch_partitions and of the events of the streaming query listeners), which holds the lock of the hook, the user context extensions, the plan compression or the last execution of its session for the moment that it reads them. The per-thread state of the other threads of the parent, of their tags and user context extensions, stays in the child, which never forgets it, as those threads end in the parent only: a leak bounded by the threads of the parent. The first call of a connection waits for the settings of the server before its request is sent, a round trip, as grpcio's does. One that does not connect fails its calls with UNAVAILABLE, which the retry policy retries, and the next call that needs a connection connects again (see What "drop-in" means for what stays different from grpcio). Before, a direct connection bounded its TCP connection only, by 10 s, the addresses of a proxy were tried in turn within one deadline, so a TLS handshake that the server never answered held a call until its deadline, and a plaintext server that never answered until the keepalive of the connection. The status of a channel that cannot connect has the client's message, of the last error of its addresses, such as failed to connect to all addresses; last error: 127.0.0.1:1: tcp connect error: Connection refused (os error 111), or of a name that does not resolve, dns error: failed to look up <host>:<port>: ..., of one whose lookup timed out, dns error: failed to look up <host>:<port>: timed out, where it was tonic's tcp connect error, and a call that the end of its connection fails has tonic's message of it. The Python binding sets the dialect of grpcio instead, with set_grpcio_dialect (not a stable API): the texts of grpcio, the User-Agent: grpc-httpcli/0.0 of grpcio's CONNECT request of a proxy, localhost and the names under it as the loopback of both families, as c-ares resolves them, and the state of gRPC's channel, whose calls fail at once once it failed to connect, while its subchannels connect again in the background with gRPC's backoff, until a call takes the connection that one made there, until 30 minutes without a call, or until its client is closed (SparkConnectClient::close), and whose resolver resolves the name of the target before the first call, and again as its connection ends or fails, no sooner than 30 s after its last resolution, as gRPC's, which a Rust client does not have, as its calls recover from an outage at once. The binding sets the end of the user agent too, python/<version> where the Rust API's is python/rust, with channel::set_user_agent_language (not a stable API either). The user agent is sent as the client type of the requests only, as PySpark sends it, and the HTTP user-agent header is tonic's own, so a user agent of a control character connects, where its connection failed. A user agent longer than 2048 characters after URL escaping, and a SPARK_CONNECT_USER_AGENT that is not UTF-8, are the error of each request that carries the user agent, as PySpark's userAgent raises them when a request is built, where the first failed the connection of the session and the second sent _SPARK_CONNECT_PYTHON in its place. The user agent of a client is read when the client is made, of the connection string, else of SPARK_CONNECT_USER_AGENT, a client of a new session keeps it, and SparkConnectClient::refresh_user_agent and SparkSession::refresh_user_agent (not a stable API) read it again, as the Python binding does as each of its calls starts. A tab, CR or LF in the connection string is removed, as urllib.parse.urlsplit removes them, where it failed. The session_id of the connection string is checked as PySpark's uuid.UUID(session_id, version=4) checks it, of Python's messages, and is sent as it is given, where it was checked as a UUID of the uuid crate, of the origin invalid UUID. A retry policy of no max_backoff_ms keeps its initial backoff, as PySpark's does, where its backoff grew by its multiplier.

    A ChannelBuilder of ChannelBuilder::with_credentials (of channel::Credentials, with ChannelBuilder::credentials and SparkSessionBuilder::channel_credentials, not a stable API), which the Python binding gives the channel of a DefaultChannelBuilder as PySpark's toChannel decides them, has those credentials in place of the rule of ChannelBuilder::secure, of use_ssl and of a token on a host that is not localhost: Insecure is plaintext and sends no token, of the connection string or of SPARK_CONNECT_AUTHENTICATE_TOKEN; Local, gRPC's local credentials, is plaintext, of the token of the connection string, over a connection whose local address is 127.0.0.1 or ::1 only, as gRPC's handshake takes it, else the call fails of its Endpoint is neither UDS or TCP loopback address.; and Ssl is TLS, of the token of the connection string. A connection string never sets them: one of a parameter of any name is as before, so the connection strings of remote(), SPARK_REMOTE and the Rust API are unchanged.

    A ChannelBuilder of ChannelBuilder::with_target (of channel::ChannelTarget, with ChannelBuilder::target, SparkSessionBuilder::channel_target and grpc_target::parse, not a stable API), which the Python binding gives the channel of a channel builder of the target of gRPC of its endpoint, connects to the name, the addresses or the Unix sockets of the target, in their order, in place of its host and its port, or fails each call of the status of a target that gRPC refuses, and of ChannelBuilder::with_metadata (with SparkSessionBuilder::channel_metadata), the binding's of the metadata() of the builder, sends that metadata in place of its parameters; and SparkConnectClient::set_metadata_source (with SparkSession::set_metadata_source and channel::MetadataSource, not a stable API) gives the calls of a client the metadata of a source, which the binding reads of a metadata() of its own for each call. A connection string sets none of them. Of the connections of every client, a connection that fails as it starts, of the error of connect, as one of a network that cannot be reached, fails of gRPC's text of it in the dialect of grpcio (FAILED_PRECONDITION: ... connect failed: addr: ... error: Network is unreachable), and in that dialect a name is resolved as c-ares takes it: an address of four decimal numbers of leading zeros and an IPv6 address of a zone are addresses, a name of a character other than [A-Za-z0-9-._/*] or of an empty label is not resolved (Misformatted domain name), and a name that the system's resolver reads as an address, as 127.1, is a name that it looks up. The name of a channel::ChannelTarget::Name of a server, the DNS server of the authority of a target of dns://, is resolved through that server, of both dialects, as gRPC's resolver resolves it (see the endpoint of a channel builder below), but through the tunnel of a proxy, whose own name the system's resolver resolves.

    GRPC_DEFAULT_SSL_ROOTS_FILE_PATH and its file are read once in a process, when its first TLS session is made, as grpcio reads them, where each client read them when it connected, and an empty file is as no file, without a warning. A TLS channel trusts the roots of that file alone, as grpcio, else those of the system's trust store, as before, and the roots of the file of client::set_bundled_ssl_roots_file (not a stable API) besides, which the Python binding sets to grpcio's bundle where grpcio is installed. A roots file without a certificate does not fail the session when it is made: each call fails UNAVAILABLE, as grpcio fails to make the handshaker of its sessions, where the session failed with Failed to configure TLS, and so does a system of no root. The core no longer enables tonic's tls-ring and tls-native-roots features, which it does not use, as it makes the TLS sessions itself: a program that uses tonic's own TLS enables them. The crates enable tonic's channel and codegen features only, of its client, where they enabled its default features, of its server (server and router) too: a program that uses tonic's server enables them. An invalid proxy address of the environment fails only the sessions of the hosts that the proxy applies to, as grpcio connects the others directly, where it failed the sessions of every host. What grpcio logs of a channel, of a proxy variable that is not a proxy, of a roots file that cannot be read, and, in the dialect of grpcio, of a TLS handshake that fails and of a proxy that refuses the tunnel, also goes to the listener of grpc_log::set_listener (a grpc_log::Listener, not a stable API), as it goes to tracing, of the grpc_log::Severity of grpcio's line, which grpc_log::Logged adds as its severity, Info or Error; the Python client writes it to stderr, as grpcio does. The client of a new session logs the variables of the proxy again, as it reads them again for its channel. RetriesListener::caught returns the error that the call fails with, of one that PySpark's except Exception does not catch, as a KeyboardInterrupt of a retry policy of the Python binding, and so SparkConnectClient::read_plan_compression returns a Result.

    A message over the size limit of a call, of 128 MiB, fails the call with RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED (8), as grpc-python's: a response, which tonic does not read, where SparkError::grpc_code was tonic's OUT_OF_RANGE (11), and a request, which the client does not send, as it checks its length before the call, where tonic's check, as it encodes the request in the body of the call, reset the stream of the call, of the error INTERNAL (13), h2 protocol error: http2 error, of no cause. The texts are tonic's (Error, decoded message length too large: found <length> bytes, the limit is: <limit> bytes, and encoded of a request), and in the dialect of grpcio those of gRPC's core (Stream removed (CLIENT: Received message larger than max (<length> vs. <limit>)), and CLIENT: Sent message larger than max (<length> vs. <limit>) of a request). As tonic checks the length of a response before any codec reads it, its status of it is told by its code, its text and the limit of the client, 134217728: a status of a server of the same code, text and limit, of no details, as a server of tonic whose own limit is 128 MiB too sends of a request over it, is read as the client's, where the status of a server of another limit, as of tonic's default of 4 MiB, is the server's; the client's own status of a request that it does not send is tagged as the client's. A TLS session is never resumed, as grpcio does not resume one by default, where rustls kept the sessions of its connections in a cache, whose lock a child of a fork() shared with its parent. grpcio's error of a response that the client fails to decode has no debug_error_string (GrpcioError::debug_error_string is an Option, not a stable API), as grpcio's, where it had the name of the code and prost's message, in the error that a retry policy of the Python binding is asked of, in the log of the errors of the calls and in the warning of a release that fails in the background. The metadata of a response, its headers, or its trailers, that reaches gRPC's hard limit of 16 KiB, of HPACK's sizes of its entries (of the name, the value and 32 bytes each), fails the call with RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED of gRPC core's text (received metadata size exceeds hard limit (value length <length> vs. 16384), of an entry whose value, or whose name, reaches it alone with 32 bytes, or (<size> vs. 16384); <entries>, of the entries as gRPC's core lists them, the headers that it knows first), where hyper's limit of a header list of 16 KiB reset the stream of the call, of INTERNAL, or ended the connection of a far larger one, of UNAVAILABLE, which the retry policy retried: hyper now takes a header list of up to 16 MiB, which the channel checks, while the connection still advertises 16 KiB as the size of a header list that it takes, as grpcio's channel does, so that a server that honours it, as grpc-java's, resets the stream of a call of more rather than sending it. A binary value (of a name ending in -bin) that gRPC's core does not decode as base64 fails the call INTERNAL, Error parsing '<name>' metadata: illegal base64 encoding, whatever the code of the server, as gRPC's core fails it, the grpc-status-details-bin of an error too, where tonic panicked of it, of which the call has the details, empty, as gRPC's core gives them the call. gRPC's core takes base64 of no padding and of any = at its end, such as YWJj=, and refuses a last group whose unused bits are not zero, such as YW==. A stream that the server resets fails its call of the code that gRPC's core gives the reason, CANCELLED of CANCEL, UNAVAILABLE of REFUSED_STREAM, RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED of ENHANCE_YOUR_CALM, PERMISSION_DENIED of INADEQUATE_SECURITY and INTERNAL of any other, where tonic's channel gave them all UNKNOWN, which retries::grpc_python_code made INTERNAL. In the dialect of grpcio, the metadata past gRPC's soft limit of 8 KiB fails its call at random too, as gRPC's core fails it, the more often the larger it is, of received metadata size exceeds soft limit (<size> vs. 8192); <entries>, of the details of the server's error, which gRPC's core still gives the call; a reset stream has gRPC core's details, Stream removed (RST_STREAM (Received RST_STREAM with error code <reason>)); and a failure whose google.rpc.Status (its grpc-status-details-bin) is not of its code or its details, as of metadata past the soft limit or of empty details, a status of the code OK, is the ValueError of grpc_status's rpc_status.from_call where PySpark's client reads the status, and one whose details upb's parser does not read as a google.rpc.Status, as of one nested past its limit, is protobuf's DecodeError (Error parsing message with type 'google.rpc.Status': Wire format was corrupt) there, and so is one whose first ErrorInfo or RetryInfo upb does not read, of its type, where PySpark unpacks it (see SparkError::grpcio, not a stable API). In the dialect of grpcio, the delay of a RetryInfo is PySpark's, signed (the ToMilliseconds() of its retry_delay, 0 of one of none), and so is the wait before a retry, of Python's numbers; retry_delay_ms and RetryPolicyState::next_attempt are unchanged, of a delay of a u64 of milliseconds, of the seconds and the nanoseconds each clamped at 0 and of a sum that saturates, which the Rust client waits as it is. The error of a closed client or channel of an execution comes once the lookup of the plan compression and the hook of the executions ran (set_execute_plan_hook), as PySpark reads its _stub once its plan is built and its hooks ran, and carries the operation ID of its request, when it has one (SparkError::operation_id), as of its other failures.

    PythonUDFPayload and LogicalPlan::CommonInlineUdtf add a pickler field, which is not a stable API: the pickling of the command of a UDF or UDTF of the Python client, which a request calls each time it builds the plan, as PySpark pickles the function then.     CoGroupedData::input_columns is removed. LocalRowIterator::next_decoded takes the next row of the batch that the iterator holds without fetching another, and LocalRowIterator::has_received_batch tells whether a record batch of the result has arrived, also one without rows.

    These raise PySpark's errors where the request was sent before: the requests of the DataFrame of a lateral_join of another join type than an inner, a left outer or a cross join raise UNSUPPORTED_JOIN_TYPE, those of a DataStreamReader given a name that PySpark's name refuses INVALID_STREAMING_SOURCE_NAME, and those of the changes of a reader of a schema _LEGACY_ERROR_TEMP_1189; Catalog::create_table_with and create_external_table_with of a schema that is not a struct raise NOT_EXPECTED_TYPE; and the await_termination and await_any_termination of a timeout that is not positive raise VALUE_NOT_POSITIVE, where they raised INVALID_TIMEOUT, which PySpark does not have, and of a NaN, an infinite or a too large timeout the errors of PySpark's int(timeout * 1000). PySpark raises the errors of lateralJoin, name and changes at the call; those builders return a DataFrame, not a Result, so the plan of the DataFrame carries the error, which fails each request of it, and the request sends nothing.

    The same way, these raise PySpark's errors where the request was sent before: the requests of the DataFrames of repartition, coalesce and repartition_by_id of a count that is not positive, and of repartition_by_expressions of a negative count, raise VALUE_NOT_POSITIVE (repartition_by_expressions of a count of 0 sends no count, as PySpark's repartition(*cols)); those of approx_quantile of a probability below 0 or above 1 raise NOT_EXPECTED_TYPE, and of a negative relative error NEGATIVE_VALUE; and those of DataStreamReader::load and of its format methods of a path that is empty once Python's strip() takes its whitespace off raise VALUE_NOT_NON_EMPTY_STR.

    DataFrame::replace, NaFunctions::replace and plan::na_replace send both strings of each pair as string literals, as PySpark's replace of strings sends them, so that a string that reads as a number, such as "1", "nan" or "inf", is replaced in the string columns. Before, such a string was sent as a double, which replaced the number in the numeric columns and left the strings as they were, or failed the request with CAST_INVALID_INPUT when the replacement was not a number. Replace numbers with replace_literals, such as replace_literals(vec![(LiteralExpression::Long(0), LiteralExpression::Long(9))], None): as PySpark's replace, it sends an integer or a float as a double, where it sent it as it was, and the requests of its DataFrame raise MIXED_TYPE_REPLACEMENT unless the values to replace and the replacements that are not null are all booleans, all strings or all numbers, of which a boolean is one too, as a Python bool is an int. NaFunctions::replace_literals does the same.

    DataFrameWriter::bucket_by of a count that is not positive does as clear_bucket_by does, as PySpark's WriteOperation sends the bucketing only of a count above 0; it sent the count, which the server refused.

    StatFunctions::corr takes the method of PySpark's corr(col1, col2, method=None): None and "" are "pearson", which the request sends, and another method is VALUE_NOT_ALLOWED; LogicalPlan::StatCorr adds its method field. DataFrame::col("*") is the star of the DataFrame, as PySpark's df["*"]. functions::reduce sends the function reduce, as PySpark's, where it sent aggregate, and reduce_with_finish is added. DataFrame::unpersist_opt takes an optional blocking, of which None sends none, as PySpark's unpersist(None). SparkSession::create_dataframe_from_arrow of batches of no rows and a schema sends the schema alone, as PySpark's empty data, where it sent the batches, which the server failed to cast to a schema of other columns. Without a schema, it sends batches of no rows with their Arrow schema, where PySpark's createDataFrame of empty data, of a table too, raises CANNOT_INFER_EMPTY_SCHEMA before it reads the schema of the table, as the Python client does: the batches carry their schema, so there is none to infer. PythonUDFPayload::function makes the payload of the function of a streaming foreach or foreachBatch sink, which, as PySpark's, sends the command and the Python version of a payload only, and not its output type and eval type. A window frame sends a bound of 0 as the current row, and a start of Preceding(i64::MAX) or before and an end of Following(i64::MAX) or after as unbounded, as PySpark's rowsBetween and rangeBetween of -sys.maxsize and sys.maxsize; an unbounded bound on the other side of the frame is the literal of its int, and an offset of a row frame beyond an int fails the request with VALUE_NOT_BETWEEN, where the server refused it.

    spark_connect::col (column::col) of "*" or "t.*" is a star, as functions::col was, as PySpark's col, and so are the names "*" and "t.*" that the methods take as Columns, by From<&str> and From<String>, such as select(["*"]), where they were attributes of those names, which the server failed with UNRESOLVED_COLUMN. WindowSpec::partition_by and WindowSpec::order_by replace the partitioning or the ordering of the spec with their columns, as PySpark's and Spark's do, where they appended them to those of the spec. The three-argument variant_set and try_variant_set send create_if_missing of true, and the tuple_union_* functions lgNomEntries of 12 and mode of "sum", the defaults that PySpark sends, where they sent no such argument.

    DataFrameReader::text and DataStreamReader::text set the option wholetext to false, also over an earlier one, as PySpark's text of its wholetext=False, so that they read lines, and a read without a schema sends an empty one, as PySpark's readers do, which the server takes as none. DataFrameWriter::mode("errorifexists") fails the write with UNSUPPORTED_OPERATION before it sends anything, as PySpark Connect's WriteOperation does, where it wrote with SAVE_MODE_ERROR_IF_EXISTS: SaveMode::from_str no longer takes errorifexists. Catalog::create_external_table and create_external_table_with send a CreateTable, of a description option as the description of the table, as PySpark's createExternalTable calls createTable, where they sent a CreateExternalTable. The scalar operations of Catalog, such as current_database, table_exists, cache_table and drop_temp_view, run as the execution of the DataFrame of their plan, of its plan ID and of the tags of the thread, as PySpark's _execute_and_fetch runs them, where they sent their relation without them. DataFrameReader::parse of a JSON schema of a type that PySpark's StructType.fromJson does not read, such as null, calendarinterval or unparsed(...), gives a DataFrame whose requests raise PySpark's CANNOT_PARSE_DATATYPE, or UNSUPPORTED_DATA_TYPE of an object of another type than an array, a map, a struct and a UDT, and send nothing, as PySpark's Parse plan raises them when it is built.

    SparkSession::create_dataframe and create_dataframe_from_columns read the settings of the session in one request, once they have converted the rows, as create_dataframe_from_arrow does, so that a row that does not convert fails without a request, and upload their data when its size reaches the server's spark.sql.session.localRelationCacheThreshold, as PySpark does, where data below 64 KiB stayed inline. When spark.sql.execution.arrow.useLargeVarTypes is true, they send the strings and binaries of the schema as large_string and large_binary, as PySpark does, and the size that each path of local data compares with the threshold is that of pyarrow's nbytes, so that a relation is inline or uploaded as PySpark's is. create_dataframe_from_columns of no rows sends the schema alone, as PySpark's empty data, where it sent a Config request and Arrow data.

    The deletion of a native ML model that is dropped is built on the thread that drops it, of its tags and user context extensions, and sent reattachable through the hooks, as PySpark's _delete_ml_cache, where a worker of the runtime built it without them. The removal of the cached relation of a checkpoint that is dropped is one execution that is not reattachable, with no hooks and no ReleaseExecute, as PySpark's __del__ sends it, where it was reattachable and released. The registration of the listener bus carries the tags of the session, as PySpark's execute_command_as_iterator. The subqueries and table arguments of a DataFrame of another session resolve, as their references are of the plan IDs of their own session, as PySpark's, where they failed with Missing relation in WithRelations.

    DataFrameWriter::jdbc and DataStreamReader::xml are added, as PySpark's jdbc of a writer and xml of a stream reader, and so are the approx_quantile, corr, cov, crosstab, freq_items and sample_by of DataFrame, which PySpark's DataFrame has besides stat, the fill_value and fill_map of NaFunctions, of the values of fillna_value and fillna_map, and Column::outer and Column::transform.

    TableArg::is_partitioned tells whether partition_by or with_single_partition has partitioned a table argument, as PySpark's TableArg._is_partitioned. DataType::class_name is the name of PySpark's class of a type, type(dt).__name__, such as LongType, by which PySpark's errors name it, and DataType::simple_string of the VectorUDT and MatrixUDT of pyspark.mllib.linalg is vector and matrix, as of those of pyspark.ml.linalg, where it was udt.

    StructField::metadata is a types::Metadata, where it was a BTreeMap<String, serde_json::Value>: a JSON object, of its keys in their order and of its values as they are written, an int beyond 64 bits and the NaN and Infinity of Python's JSON too, which a plan sends as PySpark's json.dumps(metadata) writes it, where an int beyond 64 bits was sent as a float. Metadata::new(), or Default::default(), is no metadata, and Metadata::from a BTreeMap or a serde_json::Map of serde_json values, or collect of key and value pairs, makes metadata of them, so a struct expression of metadata: BTreeMap::new() takes Metadata::new(), and one of a map takes its .into(); Metadata::from_json reads the text of a JSON object, and Metadata::json writes it. The hidden Metadata::from_json_value, which is not a stable API, reads the text of any JSON value, of which another value than an object, such as a list, has no keys and is not empty, and is written and sent as it is, as PySpark's field keeps and sends such metadata. get returns the value of a key as an owned serde_json::Value, where it returned a reference: an int beyond 64 bits as its nearest float and a float that is not finite as null, of which get_json gives the exact text. insert sets the value of a key at its place, or after the others, and returns the value it had, remove, contains_key, len and is_empty are as those of the map, and keys gives each key once in its order, where the map's were sorted. It has no iter, values, entry, get_mut, indexing (metadata["comment"]) or IntoIterator: to_map gives the map of its keys and serde_json values, to iterate in the order of the keys, or keys and get in their order, and its Debug shows its JSON. It is Clone, Default and PartialEq, as Python compares the dicts of its JSON, but not Eq, Hash, Serialize or Deserialize, which the map was. DataType::add takes a map of serde_json values, as before. The Arrow schema of SparkSession::create_dataframe and create_dataframe_from_columns of a DataType::Udt holds the metadata of each field of its sql_type, at any depth (json.dumps of it, under SPARK::metadata::json), as PySpark's to_arrow_type writes it, and that of no other field, as before, and the names of the fields of its sql_type as they are, also of duplicates, which the server then fails to read, as PySpark's, where they were deduplicated. The name of a float literal, and the JSON of a float of an ML param, are Python's repr of it, of an even last digit of a tie of its shortest digits, where a tie was rounded up (74334700308107.625 is 74334700308107.62). DataType::json, and the new StructField::json, write the metadata as it is, of its keys sorted, and each character of a string that is not printable ASCII as a \u escape, as PySpark's json() writes them, where they wrote UTF-8; json_value holds the values of the metadata as serde_json values. DataType::from_json_str and StructField::from_json_str read the JSON of Python's json.dumps, of NaN and Infinity too, of the metadata as it is written, and the detail of their INVALID_JSON is the message of their own parser.

    functions adds a form of each optional argument of a PySpark function that had none, which sends PySpark's request, such as to_date_with_format, first_with_ignore_nulls, from_json_with_options (of name and value pairs) and make_interval_with_parts (of an Option of each part).

    The client decodes the responses of the server within the limit of prost's decoder, of 100 levels of nested messages, and a deeper response is prost's error of its limit ([Internal error] failed to decode Protobuf message: ...: recursion limit reached), also in a build of prost's no-recursion-limit feature (which another crate of the build may enable, as the Python binding does), where a response decoded at any depth and one of some thousands of levels ended the process: the codec of the generated client, apache-spark-connect-proto's ClientCodec, scans the bytes of each response for the levels of their messages before prost decodes them, and so do the raw RPCs of SparkConnectClient (execute_plan_raw, reattach_execute_raw and analyze_plan_raw) and the reads of the grpc-status-details-bin of a failure. apache-spark-connect-proto adds a server feature, off by default, which generates tonic's server bindings (spark_connect_service_server), of the codec of the client: the generated server decodes the requests that it receives as prost does, of no limit of the responses. ClientCodec, of its ClientDecoder, and its ClientDecodeError, the source of the status of a response that the client fails to decode, which is_client_decode_error tells, are new, as is bytes_codec::ClientBytesCodec<U>, of its ClientBytesDecoder<U>, the BytesCodec of a client whose responses nest within the limit of the responses of the client: a deeper one fails with the status of prost's error of the limit, tagged as the client's (ClientDecodeError). So is prost::Name of each message of the proto crate, of its full name in the package spark.connect and of the type URL type.googleapis.com/spark.connect.<name>, so that prost_types::Any::from_msg packs it.

    The core also adds doc-hidden items that the Python client uses, which are not a stable API, such as the plan_build module of a PlanBuildListener of the process, WeakSparkSession, TableArg::relation_with_levels, SparkSession::add_unencodable_tag, of a tag that UTF-8 cannot encode, and StreamingQuery::await_termination_ms and StreamingQueryManager::await_any_termination_ms; doc-hidden methods of v4.3.0 change their arguments; and the doc-hidden DataFrame::exclusive_relation_owners of v4.3.0 is replaced by the doc-hidden gc module, of the references to the parts of plans and expressions that values share (gc::PartRef), which the doc-hidden held_parts of DataFrames, grouped data and cogroups, statistics, table arguments, window specifications and writers give, as a binding shows them to its garbage collector.

    LogicalPlan::Raw and SentExpression add a doc-hidden holes field (udf::SentHoles), which is not a stable API: the holes of the commands of the UDFs of a relation or an expression of a pickle of the Python client, which a request fills with the messages that their picklers give when it builds the plan, as PySpark's unpickled plan pickles the functions of its UDFs then. Code that constructs the variant or the struct directly sets it to Default::default(), of no holes, of which the relation or the expression is sent as before, and a pattern that names every field of either adds holes, or .., as a pattern without .. names every field. The doc-hidden expression::UnbuildableExpression adds an owner field so, which keeps alive what the error of the expression needs (Expression::unbuildable_owned); Expression::unbuildable gives none. The doc-hidden DataFrame::deep_copy_with_pickler_copies of another session gives a copy of the plan, of the copies of the picklers and of the Observations, whose nodes have in that session the plan IDs that they have in the session of the DataFrame, and the root plan IDs within the WithRelations of those of subqueries too, where it gave a DataFrame of the relation of the plan. The plan IDs of the core are of the one counter of the process from which the doc-hidden plan::fresh_plan_id gives the Python client the IDs of its own plans.

    SparkConnectClient::close (and so SparkSession::close and stop) closes the channel of the client, as PySpark's close closes its gRPC channel: a client that is open again after it (the doc-hidden SparkConnectClient::set_closed(false) and reopen, and SparkSession::reopen) fails each RPC with the BuiltinValueError of grpc's closed channel, Cannot invoke RPC: Channel closed! of an execution or a reattach and Cannot invoke RPC on closed channel! of the others, where it sent them, and the RPCs of the artifacts of a closed client fail with that BuiltinValueError rather than NO_ACTIVE_SESSION, and so do the reattaches and the retries of a reattachable execution that runs as its session is stopped, as by another thread, and its release, which is logged (ReleaseExecute failed with exception: Cannot invoke RPC on closed channel!.), as PySpark's iterator sends them through the stub that it was made with. As grpcio's close cancels the calls of the channel, a call of the client, or of its clones, that is in flight when it is closed, as by another thread, ends then, of CANCELLED: Channel closed! before its response begins, and Stream removed (Channel closed!) while its response is read, as of an execution whose next response the server has not sent. The stream of an execution of the client that is read after the close, as of an iterator left open across stop(), fails at its next read so too: of CANCELLED (Stream removed (Channel closed!)), and of no more of its responses, or of the ValueError of grpc_status of a failure that the server ended it with before, whose google.rpc.Status does not match the cancel, as PySpark's. SparkSession::close and stop (and so client.close() and stop() of the Python client) first wait for the releases in the background of the executions whose streams ended, at most 10 s, as PySpark's close waits for the release futures that its iterators left it, so that the close of the channel does not cancel them: a release that the server refuses, as of the session that stop() released, is logged once it is answered, and the releases of a stream that is still read are not waited for. A signal of the process that ends that wait, in the Python client, raises its exception and leaves the session open, as the KeyboardInterrupt of PySpark's wait leaves its close; the doc-hidden SparkSession::close_at_once closes at once, close_after_releases gives None when the wait was ended so, of SparkConnectClient::wait_for_releases, and has_releases_to_wait_for tells whether the wait has releases to wait for. A client that is marked closed (set_closed(true), SparkSession::mark_closed), as a response of another server-side session and INVALID_HANDLE.SESSION_CHANGED mark it, keeps its channel open: it uploads artifacts, as PySpark's ArtifactManager does, reattaches an execution that it reads, as PySpark's iterator, and sends its RPCs again once it is open. SparkConnectClient::release_session of a closed client raises NO_ACTIVE_SESSION. The client of with_new_session_id and clone_session, and so the session of SparkSession::new_session and clone_session, is of the user_id of the connection, else of the user of the requests of the client that makes it, which the doc-hidden set_request_user_id sets (the user of the artifacts stays that of the client). The doc-hidden set_thread_request_context gives the requests of the current thread a session ID, a user and global user context extensions of their own, which client_session_id, request_user_id and global_user_context_extensions give on that thread. The doc-hidden SparkConnectClient::with_new_session_id_of_user and clone_session_of_user, and SparkSession::new_session_of_user and clone_session_with_id_of_user, make the client or the session of a new session or of a clone of the user that they are given, as it is, also when the connection has a user_id, which with_new_session_id, clone_session, new_session and clone_session_with_id take in its place. The doc-hidden SparkSession::has_thread_tags tells whether the current thread has a set of tags, and SparkSession::keep_thread_request_context keeps the context of the requests of the calling thread (the session ID, the user and the global extensions of set_thread_request_context, the extensions of the thread and its tags) until the guard that it returns drops, of SparkConnectClient::thread_request_state and restore_thread_request_state. The doc-hidden spark_connect::session::set_finalizer_request_hook sets, once for the process, the FinalizerRequestHook of the functions that a session calls on the thread of a finalizer around the release of the relation of a checkpoint or of a cached ML object whose last handle dropped: before, before the request is built, which is not sent when it returns false, and after, once the thread has its context of before before again. The doc-hidden spark_connect_core::client::set_failure_requests_hook sets, once for the process, a function that a client calls on the thread that converts the failure of a call, an error of the server, which it is given, before it builds the requests of the details of the error and of the configs of its stack trace, and which returns whether to send them: when it does not, the error of the server is the error of the call as it is, and an error of the function is the error of the call in place of it. The per-thread state of a session and of its client (its tags, its user context extensions and its request context) is that of spark_connect_core::runtime::calling_thread_id: a future of a call that block_on polls on another thread, inside a current_thread runtime, reads and sets the state of the thread that calls, which ends with that thread, and the future of a Watch::Interrupt runs as a call of the thread that waits. A failed SparkConnectClient::clone_session (and so SparkSession::clone_session) raises the error of the status of its RPC without the details and the stack trace of the server, which it no longer requests, as PySpark's clone leaves its failure to grpc, and its response is not checked against the session of the client (a session ID or a server-side session ID of the response that differs is not an error, and does not close the client), as PySpark's clone does not check it; the details of a failure are not requested once the wait watch of the call returned a Watch::Interrupt. The doc-hidden spark_connect::session::Tag is a tag of a thread, which the doc-hidden SharedTags::new, replace and tags take and give: Text, Unencodable, of a str that UTF-8 cannot encode, and NotText and NotUtf8, tags that protobuf's string field does not take, with which each execution of the thread fails.

    The doc-hidden plan::BuiltOutside is a message that a client built outside the core, with the type URLs of the placeholders of DataFrames in the encoding that BuiltOutside::decoded decodes it of: the core reads the fields of its relations and commands and the messages of its extensions for placeholders only when that encoding holds a type URL that is not of a placeholder at its relations. The doc-hidden DataFrame::from_relation, SparkSession::execute_plan_proto, execute_command_proto and analyze_plan_proto take impl Into<BuiltOutside<_>>, of which a message passed as before is read everywhere, as it was; and the doc-hidden plan::resolve_request takes one and returns the request, where it changed a request in place.

    The input fields of the LogicalPlan variants (input, left, right, other, root, references and initial_input) are plan::SharedPlans, where they were Arc<LogicalPlan>s, but for the input of MlTransform, which is an Arc<LogicalPlan>, as the plan of a DataFrame is. A SharedPlan shares its node as the Arc did, and tells whether a pickler or an owner of a relation is at or below it (SharedPlan::holds_objects), as Expression::holds_objects tells of an expression, which a binding of another language reads to skip the parts of plans and expressions of none of its objects. When constructing variants directly, write SharedPlan::new(child) or .into() of a LogicalPlan or of an Arc<LogicalPlan>; arc and into_arc give the Arc, and the node dereferences as the Arc did. The plan-building helpers take impl Into<SharedPlan>, so they accept an owned LogicalPlan, a Box<LogicalPlan> or an Arc<LogicalPlan>, as before.

    Native ML objects: fetch, summaries, save and load (Rust API)¶

    spark_connect::ml reads and writes the server-side objects of Spark Connect ML as PySpark 4.3.0 does over Spark Connect, for the typed model accessors, summaries and persistence of the native classes to build on:

    • MlRef is a server-side object: a fitted model (Model::ml_ref), or another object that a fetch returns, such as a tree of an ensemble. Its clones and the DataFrames whose plans read it share it, and the last owner to drop deletes it on the server, as PySpark's RemoteModelRef. MlRef::of_reference(&session, id, release) (not a stable API) is an object that the client does not own, which the Python client makes of the RemoteModelRef that a program gives a model's constructor: its last owner runs release in place of the deletion, which releases a reference of the RemoteModelRef. MlRef::keep (not a stable API) keeps the object on the server at the drop of its last owner, as the Python client keeps the object of a model whose _java_obj is assigned another value. MlRef::release (not a stable API) deletes an object of the client on the server while its owners live, once, after which the drop of its last owner deletes nothing, as the Python client deletes the object of a model when Python finalizes the last of its Python objects (see "A model of a fit or a load" below), and MlRef::owners, MlRef::is and MlRef::is_owned (not stable APIs) count its owners, tell a clone of an owner, and tell an object of the client from one of of_reference; Model::training_summary (not a stable API) gives the target of the training summary that a model holds.
    • fetch(&MlTarget, method, args) runs a method of an object, as _call_java of pyspark.ml does over Spark Connect (a Fetch command). MlTarget::object(&model_ref).child("summary") reaches the object of a method chain ("<id>.summary"), and MlTarget::helper(&session) the ML helper (invoke_helper_attr). Its result is a value (MlValue), new objects (an id list that names the trees of an ensemble is one object per tree), or a string. When the server has lost a summary with its offloaded model, the fetch recreates the summary of the target's summary dataset (CreateSummary) and runs once more, as PySpark does.
    • MlValue is a value as deserialize_param reads it and serialize_param sends it: ML vectors and matrices, scalars, strings and arrays, byte for byte as PySpark's literals (tests/golden/ml_values.json). An empty array, or one whose first element is null, has no type to infer: send it as FetchArg::Typed, as PySpark sends a (value, DataType) pair.
    • attribute_relation(&MlTarget, method, args) is the DataFrame of a method, as invoke_remote_attribute_relation reads it, of the summary dataset of a summary target. It keeps the object and the plans of its DataFrame arguments alive.
