It builds the relations of its DataFrame arguments at the call, as PySpark's serialize, and its requests build the relation of the summary dataset, as PySpark's AttributeRelation builds the plan of its child; the requests of the DataFrame of a transform build the relation of its input, as TransformerRelation.
    • write_operator and write_object save an estimator, transformer or evaluator, or a fitted model (Write), and read loads one of its JVM class (Read), as RemoteMLWriter and RemoteMLReader of pyspark.ml.connect.readwrite. A loaded model is a new MlRef.
    • Model::write(), Estimator::write() and Evaluator::write() return an MlWriter, as PySpark's write() returns an MLWriter: .overwrite(), .option(key, value) and .save(path). A model saves of the session that holds it. An estimator or an evaluator, which holds no session, saves of the one that .session(&spark) sets (as MLWriter.session), since the Rust client has no active session.
    • The fit of the Estimator trait returns a Box<dyn Model>; model.downcast_ref::<M>() gives its concrete model type. Model::describe() is the server's description of a model (PySpark's __repr__ of a model, its toString). PredictionModel gives num_features() and predict(&vector), the members of every prediction model of pyspark.ml. PySpark reads a fetched int as a Python int of any size: num_features() (and the other i32 members) of one out of the range of an i32 is an error, not the truncated int.
    • The server sends a warning with the model of a fit when the memory control of its ML cache stopped the training of a tree model early, as the model outgrew 2/5 of the session's spark.connect.session.connectML.mlCache.memoryControl.maxModelSize (The random forest training stops early because the model size (35016 bytes) exceeds threshold (24000 bytes).; the warnings of a fit a line each, of a boosting tree also the forest's of its tree). PySpark's try_remote_fit logs it, to the logger pyspark.ml.util at WARNING. The Rust API, which has no Python logging, keeps it on the model: Model::fit_warning() (and MlRef::fit_warning()), None of a fit without one, a load and a fetch, and of a pipeline model, a OneVsRest model and a validator's model, whose stages, class models and best model have theirs. SparkSession::set_ml_fit_warning_handler sets the handler that each fit of the session calls with its warning, on the thread of the fit (of the pool of a OneVsRest), once the result of the fit has arrived and before the model is made of it, as PySpark logs it; an error of the handler is the error of the fit. The Python client logs the warnings by it.
    • A fit, transform, evaluate and save sends the params as Params::sendable serializes them, as PySpark's serialize_ml_params does: of the error of a value that PySpark cannot send, before any request. Params::set_param_int_array (of VectorSlicer::set_indices and QuantileDiscretizer::set_num_buckets_array) sends a list of ints as PySpark's literal of it, of the type of its first element (Integer when it fits 32 bits, else Long) of every element. Of a first element that fits 32 bits and another that does not, PySpark's LiteralExpression fails its assert of the element's range: the list is kept, for its getter, and is that AssertionError.
    • ObjectModel is a model of a server-side object, of its operator and params, which transforms as every cached model does: the model of an object that read_model loads, or of one that a fetch returns. training_summary(&model_ref, &dataset) is the target of the training summary of a fitted model, of its summary dataset (the training dataset itself for the models whose _summary_dataset returns it, the classification models and the linear regressions; summary_transform of it, the transform of the dataset by the model of no params, for the others, as PySpark's default _summary_dataset). evaluated_summary is the target of the summary of an evaluate, of the id that the server returns, which the client does not release, as PySpark does not.

    Native ML classes of the full pyspark.ml API (Python)¶

    The native Python classes of pyspark.ml.connect follow the API of pyspark.ml 4.3.0 over Spark Connect in full, one for each class of the __all__ of its classification, clustering, evaluation, feature, fpm, recommendation, regression and tuning modules, and for Pipeline and PipelineModel (Evaluator and ParamGridBuilder are PySpark's own).

    They live in the package of PySpark's deprecated pyspark.ml.connect (deprecated since Spark 4.0, of pandas and PyTorch training), as pyspark.ml itself stays PySpark's vendored classes over Spark Connect, and replace its classes of the same names: LogisticRegression, StandardScaler, MaxAbsScaler, CrossValidator and Pipeline (and their models), and its RegressionEvaluator, BinaryClassificationEvaluator and MulticlassClassificationEvaluator. So the package and its modules have the native classes and an __all__ of them, but for pyspark.ml.connect.pipeline, the module of Pipeline and PipelineModel, which has none (PySpark's pyspark.ml.connect exports Estimator, Evaluator, Model, Pipeline, PipelineModel, Transformer and its modules, which have no __all__); the constructors take the params of pyspark.ml, so the PyTorch params of the deprecated LogisticRegression (numTrainWorkers, batchSize, learningRate, momentum and seed) raise the TypeError of an unexpected keyword argument; and they fit, transform and evaluate Spark DataFrames only, so a pandas DataFrame raises the AttributeError of its sparkSession, where the deprecated classes fit it. The base classes Estimator, Transformer, Model and Evaluator of the package, its ArrayAssembler, its summarizer and its other helper modules stay PySpark's deprecated ones, unchanged. Each class has, where its PySpark class has them:

    • The Params API of pyspark.ml.param.Params, as PySpark's own code: params, getParam, hasParam, isSet, isDefined, hasDefault, getOrDefault, explainParam(s), extractParamMap, set and copy, the getters and setters, setParams and the keyword_only constructors are PySpark's own functions, over PySpark's param maps (_paramMap and _defaultParamMap) in the object's attributes. So their errors, messages and keywords are PySpark's, _set, _setDefault, _copyValues, _resetUid and _input_kwargs behave as PySpark's, values are converted by PySpark's TypeConverters (a NumPy string stays as given, an np.float32 raises PySpark's TypeError of toFloat), extractParamMap() is in PySpark's order (the order that the params were set; the defaults of a new object in the order that its constructors set them), and an assignment of uid keeps the Params of the old uid, as PySpark's does. The maps of the objects that this client makes are in PySpark's order too: of the constructor of the class (the defaults in the order of its MRO, which is not of their names) of a model of no server-side object, of the model of an object that a fetch returns (a tree of an ensemble, toLocal), of a model of a class method (StringIndexerModel.from_labels), of the Bucketizer of a QuantileDiscretizer's fit (of its keywords), and of the model of a validator's or a OneVsRest's fit; of the model of a JVM estimator's fit as PySpark's try_remote_fit makes them, whose _resetUid rebuilds the defaults of the model's constructor in the order of the names of the params and whose _copyValues then adds the estimator's defaults that the model's class does not set and sets the estimator's params, each of their names (an ALSModel's blockSize and predictionCol, and then ALS's coldStartStrategy, itemCol and userCol); and of a copy of Params.copy, of the names. The defaults of a load are those of its class's constructor, of the names, as _resetUid rebuilds them. The set params of a load are of their names too, a deterministic choice: PySpark's _set of a load follows the server's map of them, which upb's iteration of a map gives in an order that differs from one process to another (the set params of a load of PySpark's Python format, a OneVsRest's or a validator's, are in the order of its metadata, as PySpark's). clear does nothing over Spark Connect, as PySpark's JavaParams.clear, of any positional arguments. The native object's params are made those of the maps before each native member reads them (a fit, a transform, an evaluation, a writer, a model's members), and so are those of the objects that it holds (an OneVsRest's classifier, a Pipeline's stages, a validator's estimator, evaluator and the values of its grids, a PipelineModel's stages, an OneVsRestModel's models, a validator model's best and sub-models). A writer reads them at its save, as PySpark's RemoteMLWriter reads those of its _instance. The maps of an object that this client makes (of a fit, a load, a native copy) are made of the native object's values at their first read. The native members are functions of PySpark's names, qualified names, modules, docstrings, signatures and annotations, of the class of PySpark's that defines the member (JavaMLWritable.write, JavaPredictionModel.predict), and so are the getters of the native properties (LinearSVCModel.coefficients, JavaPredictionModel.numFeatures, _ClassificationSummary.accuracy of a summary), the __repr__ and __str__ of a class (Identifiable.__repr__, JavaModel.__repr__) and the __del__ of a Java wrapper (JavaWrapper.__del__, which releases a reference of the RemoteModelRef of a model of one, see "A model of a RemoteModelRef" below, and raises the AttributeError of a deleted _java_obj, which Python reports as unraisable in it, as PySpark's; that of a model of a fit or a load, and of its training summary, releases the model's object as PySpark's does, see "A model of a fit or a load" below), of the globals of PySpark's function, of which typing.get_type_hints resolves their annotations (-> "LinearSVCModel"). They are not PySpark's code, of which inspect.getsource gives none, nor wrappers of it: where PySpark's function is the wrapper of a decorator (try_remote_attribute_relation, try_remote_write), they have no __wrapped__. Python binds a call of one as it binds PySpark's function, of the parameters of the wrapper of its decorator (JavaParams.clear of try_remote_intercept takes any positional arguments, a DataFrame method of try_remote_attribute_relation any arguments): a call that does not bind raises PySpark's message (JavaMLWritable.write() takes 1 positional argument but 2 were given) before the native member runs. So do the class methods and the static methods, of PySpark's kind (read, of JavaMLReadable.read and the try_remote_read of a Pipeline's or a validator's, StringIndexerModel.from_labels, StopWordsRemover.loadDefaultStopWords), whose message of a class method counts its cls (JavaMLReadable.read() takes 1 positional argument but 2 were given). Such a function, which runs the native member of a reference to it of its class's and its own names, pickles of cloudpickle by value (as a property of one, a bound method of one, and a closure or a functools.partial of one, the function of a UDF), where PySpark's pickle by reference; a plain pickle of one of the class (pickle.dumps(LinearSVCModel.summary)), which pickles a function by its qualified name, finds PySpark's function there, and raises PicklingError (... it's not the same object as pyspark.ml.classification.LinearSVCModel.summary), where PySpark's pickles. The objects have one member of the native classes that PySpark's do not, which dir() lists: __setstate__ of an object of params, which takes the state of a copy and of a pickle, of the objects that the native object holds of a PipelineModel, a OneVsRestModel and a validator's model (stages, models, bestModel, ...), and the state of the pickles of this client's earlier versions (the uid, params and defaults of the native object); and on Python 3.9 and 3.10, whose object has no __getstate__, __getstate__ too, which gives PySpark's state of the object. The public class reaches the native state of an object by functions of the native module of its native class (_ml_params, _ml_sync, _ml_java_obj, ...), of no member. The attributes that PySpark's constructor, fit or load sets of each object (uid, _paramMap, _defaultParamMap, _params, _java_obj, _summary) are of the object, as PySpark's, not of the class: of an object that this client makes (of a fit, a load, a native copy), they are made at their first read (but the _java_obj of a model, there from its making), by the __getattribute__ of the native class, which looks an attribute up as object.__getattribute__ does (of the error of a getter that raises AttributeError, raised once) and makes one of them that it does not find (see "The attributes of the objects that this client makes" below); the native class has no __setattr__ nor __delattr__, so object.__setattr__ of an object of a subclass sets its attribute, as of PySpark's. The client makes such an object of its native params, which the native code that makes it may set after it makes it, and of which a copy of the native state sends no request; dir() of the object lists them, but _summary before its first read and a deleted _java_obj (see there). The class has PySpark's signature of its own __new__, and no __signature__, as PySpark's has none; a class of no Java wrapper (a Pipeline, a OneVsRest, a validator and their models) has no __del__, as PySpark's. The Param objects are PySpark's own pyspark.ml.param.Param, of the docs, converters and defaults of the official class (crates/pyspark-rs/src/ml/ml_params.json, captured from pyspark-client 4.3.0 by scripts/capture_ml_api_golden.py), and a getter per param (getMaxIter).
    • PySpark's types and class Params: the class of pyspark.ml.connect.<module> is a subclass of its native class and of PySpark's class of the same name of pyspark.ml.<module> (python/pyspark/ml/_native_classes.py), so its objects are instances of every class of the MRO of PySpark's class: MLWritable, MLReadable and Identifiable, HasTrainingSummary and the summary bases, the param mixins (HasMaxIter), PredictionModel and ClassificationModel, PySpark's Java wrappers, and PySpark's class itself. Every object that this client makes (of a fit, a load, a copy, a summary) is of that class, which is a subclass of the class of the same module of each base of PySpark's class of the module (LinearSVCSummary of LinearSVCTrainingSummary, and both BinaryLogisticRegressionSummary and LogisticRegressionTrainingSummary of BinaryLogisticRegressionTrainingSummary), of PySpark's metaclass (type of the summaries). So isinstance(LinearSVCModel(), MLWritable) holds, and a Pipeline of pyspark.ml fits and saves native stages. A summary constructs as PySpark's JavaWrapper.__init__(java_obj=None), PySpark's own function, with its errors of a call that does not bind: it keeps the java_obj in the summary's attributes (_java_obj, also of the super().__init__ of a subclass), which its members read at each call as PySpark's _extract_id_methods: the id of an object of the ML cache and its methods ("<id>.summary") of a string, over the active session, and PySpark's AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'split' of a summary of no object. A DataFrame member (predictions, residuals()) of a summary of the summary method first, as PySpark names the training summary of a fit, reads the plan of its _summary_dataset, which PySpark's fit sets, as the summary dataset of the relation, and raises the AttributeError of the attribute of a summary of none, as PySpark's invoke_remote_attribute_relation does. The DataFrame of such a member, and of a transform, builds the relation of the summary dataset or of the transformed DataFrame at each of its requests (its schema, columns, repr, explain and actions), and pickles the functions of its UDFs then, as PySpark's AttributeRelation and TransformerRelation build the plan of their child: so a function that does not pickle fails those requests, each again, and not the member or the transform. The DataFrame arguments of a method (ChiSquareTest.test(dataset, ...), approxNearestNeighbors(dataset, ...), evaluate(dataset)) are built at the call, as PySpark's serialize builds them. The Param of a class (LinearSVC.maxIter) is PySpark's, of the parent undefined, so ParamGridBuilder().addGrid(LinearSVC.maxIter, ...) builds. The AttributeError of a missing attribute names the class as PySpark's does, of an object as of the class itself (type object 'DecisionTreeRegressor' has no attribute 'maxIter'). MLWriters and MLReaders are RemoteMLWriter and RemoteMLReader, as over Spark Connect.
    • They are plain objects of the attributes of PySpark's __init__: _sparkSession, shouldOverwrite, optionMap and _instance of a writer (RemoteMLWriter(instance)), and _sparkSession and _clazz of a reader (RemoteMLReader(clazz)), which PySpark's own session, sparkSession, overwrite and option set and read. So they construct, take any value of an attribute, and copy, copy deep and pickle at every protocol as PySpark's, of their class and attributes; as no module has the drop-in's class by its name (pyspark.ml.connect.readwrite.RemoteMLWriter is PySpark's), a pickle of one names the native module's _new_remote_ml_writer (_new_remote_ml_reader), where PySpark's names copyreg.__newobj__ of the class.
    • Their classes are subclasses of PySpark's (isinstance(w, RemoteMLWriter) holds), not PySpark's classes themselves (type(w) is RemoteMLWriter is false), of the native classes.
    • Their save and load are functions of PySpark's names, docstrings and signatures (RemoteMLWriter.save, RemoteMLReader.load), which bind a call as PySpark's do, assert an active session first (also of a model, whose Write the session that holds it sends), and read the attributes at the call, as PySpark's do: a writer saves its _instance, of its params as of the save, and a reader loads its _clazz; another object or class than a native one is saved and loaded by PySpark's own saveInstance and loadInstance, with their errors (NotImplementedError: Unsupported write for <class 'NoneType'>). The Write of an estimator, a transformer, an evaluator or a model takes shouldOverwrite and optionMap as its protobuf fields take them: an int as a bool, None as an unset overwrite (false) and as no options, a dict (or pairs) of strings, and upb's error of anything else (TypeError: bad argument type for built-in operation). A save of PySpark's Python format overwrites of the truth of shouldOverwrite, of the ML helper's handleOverwrite of the value as it is, and the writer of a validator's model reads its persistSubModels of optionMap, as PySpark's do.
    • Their save and load (and the object's) take a path as PySpark's do. A path of an estimator, a model or an evaluator is the protobuf field of its Write or Read: bytes of their UTF-8, None the empty path (which the server refuses), and anything else (a pathlib.Path too) upb's TypeError: bad argument type for built-in operation. A path of the Python format of a Pipeline or a validator is that of its os.path.join(path, "metadata"): an os.PathLike (a pathlib.Path), and the TypeError of anything else.
    • A Python subclass's load reads the JVM class of its own name, as PySpark's RemoteMLReader names it (the module, of org.apache.spark for pyspark, and the name), which the server refuses (CONNECT_ML.UNSUPPORTED_EXCEPTION); a Pipeline's or a validator's loads its class.
    • PySpark's defaults of each instance: the default seed is the hash of the name of the class, and the default outputCol the uid of the instance and __output; a fitted model has the defaults of its estimator, which PySpark's _copyValues copies to it (a seed of the name of the estimator's class), and a loaded object keeps the defaults of the uid that it was made of, as PySpark's _resetUid keeps them.
    • The members of the model: its properties and methods (coefficients, numFeatures, predict(vector), clusterCenters(), ...), which read the server's model object as PySpark's _call_java does, its setters, and __repr__, the server's description of the model. The __str__ of an RFormulaModel names its resolved formula, of the server, as PySpark's does (RFormulaModel(ResolvedRFormula(label=y, terms=[x], hasIntercept=true)) (uid=...)). Its DataFrame methods (LDAModel.describeTopics, Word2VecModel.getVectors and findSynonyms, the LSH models' approxNearestNeighbors and approxSimilarityJoin, ALSModel's recommendFor*, and the residuals of a GeneralizedLinearRegressionSummary) send the positional arguments of a call only, as PySpark's try_remote_attribute_relation does over Spark Connect: they drop keyword arguments (describeTopics(maxTermsPerTopic=1) describes the server's 10 terms, residuals(residualsType="pearson") gives the deviance residuals), and check no arity, so a call of other arguments than the server's method takes fails on the server when the DataFrame is analyzed. The model of an object that a fetch returns (a tree of an ensemble, toLocal) is of the constructor of its class, as PySpark's DecisionTreeClassificationModel(RemoteModelRef(...)): of a new uid, of its defaults and no set params.
    • _java_obj and _call_java: a model of a server-side object (of a fit, a load, a copy, a fetch or a class method such as StringIndexerModel.from_labels) has PySpark's _java_obj, the RemoteModelRef of its object (the one that it was made of, of LinearSVCModel(RemoteModelRef(id)), see "A model of a RemoteModelRef" below), and a summary the id of its object and methods ("<id>.summary" of a training summary, the server's id of the summary of an evaluate), so PySpark's own _call_java(name, *args) of either sends PySpark's Fetch (try_remote_call). A model with a training summary has PySpark's _summary, the summary. A training summary has the attributes that PySpark's try_remote_fit sets, in its order: _java_obj, _summary_dataset (a DataFrame of the summary dataset) and _remote_model_obj, the model's _java_obj itself, of a reference of its own (add_ref(), so _ref_count is 2 after a fit, as PySpark's), and the summary of an evaluate those of PySpark's evaluate: _java_obj and __source_transformer__, the model. The copies of a model share its RemoteModelRef, as PySpark's copy.copy and Params.copy share the _java_obj of its attributes, also of a copy made before either reads it. PySpark's Pipeline, OneVsRest, validators and their models are no Java wrappers, of no _java_obj, and neither are the native ones.
    • The attributes of the objects that this client makes: the attributes of an object that PySpark's constructor makes (its param maps, Params, _params and uid, of Params.__init__, and _input_kwargs and _java_obj) are in vars() from its __init__, as PySpark's, and so are those of a PipelineModel, a OneVsRestModel and a validator's model of their constructors (but the objects that the constructors take, native properties, see "Native validators"). Those of an object that this client makes in Rust (of a fit, a load, a native copy, a fetch) are made at their first read: its param maps, Params, _params and uid at the first read of one of them (by any member of the Params API, a native member, a copy or a pickle; a read of the uid alone reads the native object's), and its _summary at its first read, which makes the training summary object (of a request of numClasses of a logistic regression, as PySpark's fit makes it), so vars() of a fitted model, and inspect.getattr_static of it, lack them until then, where PySpark's fit and load set them, and dir() of it lists _summary from then on (Params.params reads every attribute that dir() lists, and so makes no summary). The state of a copy or a pickle has the param maps and lacks _summary until its first read, so a pickle of a model of a training summary and of no server-side object (of a deleted _java_obj) pickles, where PySpark's fails at the session of its summary dataset. The _java_obj of a model is in vars() from its making, as PySpark's. Their values are those of PySpark's whenever they are read (of the order of PySpark's maps, see above), but the Params of an object whose uid was assigned before they were made, which are of the uid then, where PySpark's are of the uid of the fit or the load, and do not belong to the object (ValueError: Param ... does not belong to ... of its getters). They are not made at the fit or the load, which makes the objects of many classes in Rust of their native params, so that a fit or a fetch (of the trees of an ensemble) reads no params of an object for vars() alone. The attributes are the state of the object, as PySpark's: an assignment or a deletion of the uid or of _java_obj (also of object.__setattr__, object.__delattr__ or a write of __dict__) is of the native object at its next use (see "A model of a RemoteModelRef"), and a deleted _java_obj raises PySpark's AttributeError, is not listed by dir(), and is deleted in the copies and the pickles of the object, as PySpark's; one of the others deleted after it was made is made again at its next read, where PySpark's raises the AttributeError.
    • Summaries: the training summary of a fit (summary, hasSummary), the same object on every call as PySpark's _summary, and the summary of evaluate; a loaded model has none, and raises PySpark's RuntimeError. Their DataFrames (predictions, roc, cluster, ...) are of the summary dataset of the fit. The __repr__ of a GeneralizedLinearRegressionTrainingSummary is the server's description of it (its table of coefficients and its deviances), as PySpark's is. Their members have PySpark's docstrings, and their methods PySpark's signatures: a member is a property of a property of PySpark's (r2), and a method of a method (fMeasureByLabel(beta=1.0)).
    • Fits of param maps: fit(dataset, paramMap) fits a copy of the estimator with the map, a list or tuple of maps fits once per map, and fitMultiple(dataset, paramMaps) returns PySpark's _FitMultipleIterator. transform(dataset, paramMap) transforms of a copy of the model.
    • The warning of a fit: the warning that the server sends with the model of a fit, of a tree training that the memory control of its ML cache stopped early (see "Native ML objects: fetch, summaries, save and load (Rust API)"), is logged as PySpark's try_remote_fit logs it: _logger.warning(warning_msg) of pyspark.ml.util, to the logger pyspark.ml.util at WARNING, of the server's message as it is, of a record of that line of pyspark/ml/util.py (its pathname, lineno and funcName wrapped), once the result of the Fit has arrived and before the model is made of it (before the trees of a forest are read). So each fit logs its own: of a param map, of each stage of a Pipeline, of each class of a OneVsRest, on the thread that fits it, and of each fit of a validator. An exception of the logging (of a filter that raises) is the exception of the fit, as PySpark's.
    • Persistence: write() (an MLWriter of pyspark.ml.util, with overwrite(), option, optionMap, session and save), save(path), read() (an MLReader) and load(path), as RemoteMLWriter and RemoteMLReader save and load them, so that this client and PySpark load what each other saves.
    • Subclasses: a Python subclass of a class behaves as a subclass of PySpark's class.
    • Its __init__ passes its keywords to super().__init__(...), which sets them as PySpark's keyword_only __init__ does (by setParams, recording _input_kwargs), of any arguments of its own. A call that does not bind raises PySpark's message (LogisticRegression.__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument 'foo', JavaModel.__init__() takes from 1 to 2 positional arguments but 3 were given).
    • An object of a subclass of an estimator, transformer or evaluator, or of a model class of PySpark's JavaModel, has a uid of the subclass's name (Identifiable._randomUID), and the defaults of that uid and name (outputCol, seed), and so does an object of the constructor of a subclass of PipelineModel, OneVsRestModel or a validator's model (MyPipelineModel(stages)). The models of a fit are of their base class, of its name, as PySpark's.
    • fit, fitMultiple, transform, evaluate, save and load are PySpark's own (of Estimator, Transformer, Evaluator, MLWritable and MLReadable), with PySpark's signatures and docstrings. They run the object's _fit, _transform, _evaluate, copy, write and read, so a subclass's _fit runs, also in the copies that a Pipeline or a CrossValidator fits.
    • A copy is of the class of the object, of its attributes, as PySpark's Params.copy makes it of copy.copy.
    • uid is an attribute of the object (vars() has it), which an assignment sets, as PySpark's: it keeps the Params of the old uid, which then do not belong to the object (until its _resetUid), so the getters, isSet and explainParam of a Param raise PySpark's ValueError (... does not belong to ...), and the setters and the calls of a name set and read the maps. It is a native property over the attribute, whose value the native members send: del obj.uid raises AttributeError: property has no deleter, where PySpark's deletes the attribute (and its next read raises the AttributeError of a missing attribute).
    • A fit, transform, evaluate and save send every param of _paramMap, as PySpark's serialize_ml_params: a Param of the subclass that is set too, of the literal of its value, which the server refuses (Param myParam does not exist.), as PySpark's; a Param of only a default is not sent. The error of a value of no literal, of the first param of _paramMap that cannot be sent, is raised before any request.
    • A save of a subclass of a OneVsRest, a Pipeline, a validator or a model of one writes the class of the object in its metadata (__main__.MyPipeline), as PySpark's DefaultParamsWriter does, and the Params of the subclass: the params of its extractParamMap() (of a OneVsRest and a validator, but the classifier, the estimator, the evaluator and the grid, which are saved of their own; a Pipeline's are its stageUids and language), and the defaults of its _defaultParamMap, as PySpark's writers write them. So the loads of such a file are PySpark's: the readers of a OneVsRest and a Pipeline load it of their own class, those of a validator fail of PySpark's TypeError (they read the grid of a class of no pyspark.ml. as of the JVM's writer), and a stage of a pipeline loads of its class (an AttributeError of a class that its module does not have).
    • copy.copy, copy.deepcopy and pickle take an object as PySpark's do:
    • An estimator, a transformer, an evaluator, a Pipeline or a validator, and a model of no server-side object, copy, copy deep and pickle, at every protocol, of their uid, params and attributes. A shallow copy shares the values (a Pipeline's stages), and a deep copy and a pickle copy them. Their state (__getstate__) is PySpark's, the attributes of the object (__dict__, of its param maps and its uid), as object.__getstate__ gives it (of a subclass of slots, the pair of the attributes and the slots), which __setstate__ takes as copy._reconstruct and the unpickler take PySpark's, of __dict__.update. As PySpark's class has no __copy__, copy.copy (and Params.copy, of copy.copy), copy.deepcopy and pickle of an object of a Python subclass of its own __reduce__, __reduce_ex__, __getnewargs__, __getstate__, __setstate__ or slots, or of a class of a reducer of copyreg.dispatch_table, run them as of PySpark's subclass: a subclass whose __getstate__ drops a lock of __dict__ (and whose __setstate__ makes it again) keeps its uid, params and attributes, alone and in a Pipeline. __setstate__ also takes the state of the pickles of this client's earlier versions (the uid, the set params and the own defaults of the object by name, and its attributes).
    • copy.copy copies of the reduce, as of PySpark's objects, which have no __copy__: an object of the class's __new__, of no __init__, of the attributes of the object (its param maps, and the stages, models, bestModel, ... of a PipelineModel, a OneVsRestModel and a validator's model, which their state carries after the others, as PySpark's attributes); a model of a server-side object is copied of the same object, of the native copy that its reduce names after a lock (_column_reduce.unpickled_after), at which its pickle and its deep copy fail, as PySpark's fail at the lock of its RemoteModelRef. So a failed deep copy of such a model makes no object, where PySpark's makes one of the class's __new__ before it fails at the lock of the state, whose __del__ prints the unraisable AttributeError of its read of _java_obj. A model of a server-side object of a Python subclass of a reduce, a state or slots of its own (as above) reduces as PySpark's, of object.__reduce_ex__ and the attributes of the object, which run the subclass's members: its copy is of its class and slots, a state of its own (one that drops _java_obj) copies and pickles of that state, and a failed deep copy prints the unraisable error of PySpark's. A summary is copied of the class's __new__ and its attributes, whose members read its _java_obj, as PySpark's.
    • A model of a server-side object, and its summary, copy, sharing the object, and are not copied deep nor pickled, nor is a DataFrame that keeps such a model: PySpark's raise the error of the first object that does not pickle, of the pickle of the lock of the model's RemoteModelRef at the protocol (a PicklingError below 2, the TypeError cannot pickle '_thread.lock' object from 2), or of the session of a DataFrame, which PySpark pickles after its plan (the AttributeError of its local function). A transform of a model, a DataFrame that reads one (also only through a subquery of its expressions, exists(), scalar() or isin(df), as the references of PySpark's plan keep the plans of its subqueries, after its child), and a DataFrame of a method of a model or of the summary of an evaluate keep the model in their plans, before their sessions; the summary of an evaluate keeps its model (__source_transformer__). A training summary pickles its summary dataset first, which keeps the model when it is a transform of a model (the transform of the training dataset by the model, of the KMeans, BisectingKMeans and GaussianMixture summaries, and a training dataset of a stage after a model of a Pipeline), and else raises the AttributeError of the session of the training DataFrame, and so does a DataFrame of a method of the summary (summary.predictions). Cloudpickle pickles the session, of its function by value, and so raises the TypeError of the lock of the model of the summary (_remote_model_obj) then. copy.deepcopy raises the TypeError of the lock, after PySpark's requests of the schemas of the DataFrames that it copies on the way (the lookup of __deepcopy__ of PySpark's DataFrame reads its columns).
    • So are the objects that keep the plan of such a DataFrame, as PySpark's pickle reaches the plan in them: a Column of a subquery of it (exists(), scalar(), isin(df), also within other expressions) and its expression (Column._expr), a table argument of it (asTable(), also partitioned, ordered or of a single partition) and its _subquery_expr, the plan of the DataFrame (DataFrame._plan), and its grouped data, cogrouped data and writers (groupBy, cogroup, write, writeTo), of which PySpark pickles the plan before the session. Their pickle raises PySpark's error of the lock of the model, or that of the session of a training summary's _summary_dataset (of pickle) and of the lock (of cloudpickle), and copy.deepcopy the TypeError of the lock; copy.copy copies them, sharing the model. Cogrouped data pickle their two grouped data in their order, each of its DataFrame, of its plan and then of its session: a plain DataFrame first fails at its session (the AttributeError of its local function), at every protocol, before the model of the second, and a deep copy requests the schemas of their DataFrames before the TypeError of the lock, as PySpark's.
    • The pickle of a plan or an expression reaches the functions of its UDFs and the objects of pyspark.ml that it keeps in PySpark's order of their plan objects: of a plan, its first input (_child), then the plans of the subqueries of its expressions (_references), its other inputs (the right side of a join), its expressions, of the functions of their UDFs, and last the objects that it keeps; of a grouped map (applyInPandas, applyInArrow, applyInPandasWithState), its grouping columns before its function, of a cogroup its other side between them, and of a transformWithState its initial state and its grouping columns after its function; of a Column, its expression in its order, the plan of a subquery where it is. So a plain pickle fails at the lambda of a UDF that comes before the model (df.select(udf_of_a_lambda("x")).where(transformed.exists()), (udf_of_a_lambda("x") > 0) & transformed.exists()), and at the lock of the model where the model comes first (transformed.select(udf_of_a_lambda("x"))), as PySpark's, and cloudpickle, which pickles the lambda, at the lock.
    • The DataFrames of the models of PySpark's own pyspark.ml classes, which build PySpark's plans over the drop-in's DataFrames (pyspark.ml.regression.LinearRegression, where the drop-in's own classes are those of pyspark.ml.connect), are refused as PySpark's: a DataFrame of such a plan (DataFrame(plan, session)) reaches what PySpark's pickle of the plan reaches, of the models and the summaries that the plan keeps (__source_transformer__, __source_instance__), its child first (the plan of a DataFrame of the drop-in, of the functions of its UDFs, at which a plain pickle fails first). So a transform of such a model, and a DataFrame of a method of it or of the summary of its evaluate, raise the error of the lock of the model, and a DataFrame of a method of its training summary the AttributeError of the session of the summary's _summary_dataset (of pickle) and the TypeError of the lock of its _remote_model_obj (of cloudpickle and copy.deepcopy), or the lock of a summary dataset that is a transform of the model (of a clustering model).
    • A summary of PySpark's constructor (LinearRegressionSummary("<id>.summary")), of no server-side object, copies, copies deep and pickles, at every protocol, as PySpark's plain object: of its class and of its attributes (_java_obj).
    • A shallow copy of a model keeps the server-side object while either object lives (it holds the object as a Params.copy does, see "A model of a fit or a load" below). PySpark's copy.copy shares its RemoteModelRef without a reference of its own, and the __del__ of each object releases one, which deletes the server-side object at a count of 0: the collection of one copy of a model of no training summary deletes the object of the original (CONNECT_ML.CACHE_INVALID at its next use), and that of a model with one, whose summary holds a reference too, survives one copy's collection and not two (of copies of the model or of its summary), where this client's original keeps working. A copy of a Python subclass of a reduce of its own, which copies as PySpark's of the attributes of the object (see above), holds the RemoteModelRef of its _java_obj as a model of it, and so releases it as PySpark's copy does.
    • A model of a fit or a load: as PySpark's JavaWrapper.__del__ releases the reference of a model, of each Params.copy of it and of its training summary (_remote_model_obj) of the model's RemoteModelRef when Python finalizes the object, the __del__ of each of these objects releases its hold of the model's server-side object (and so of a tree of an ensemble and of another object that a fetch returns), and that of the last of them deletes the object: when its last reference goes, and when the garbage collector finalizes a reference cycle of them, before it breaks the cycle. So a model or a summary that a finalizer of such a cycle saves (resurrects) has lost its object, and its members, the members of its summary and its transforms raise CONNECT_ML.CACHE_INVALID at their next requests, as PySpark's; an explicit __del__ of a model of no training summary deletes its object at once. The object is deleted once, and not again when the objects go. A transform of the model, a DataFrame of a member of the model or of its summary, the summary of an evaluation, a writer and a call that runs hold the object, as PySpark's hold the model or its summary (__source_transformer__, __source_instance__): the last of them deletes the object when it goes, if the objects of the model were finalized before (see dev/parity/known-differences.md).
    • A model of no server-side object: a model class constructs as PySpark's JavaModel(java_model=None) (KMeansModel(), LDAModel()), of PySpark's JavaModel.__init__, a model of a new uid of its class and of its params, none set, whose Params API, copy and write() work, and whose members of the server, __repr__, a transform and a save raise PySpark's bare AssertionError, as does the params walk of a model of functools.cached_property members (trees), and so its explainParams and copy.
    • A model of a RemoteModelRef: a model class constructs of the RemoteModelRef of a server-side object as PySpark's JavaModel(java_model) over Spark Connect (LinearSVCModel(RemoteModelRef(id))): a model of a new uid of its class and of its params, none set, whose _java_obj is the reference, of no reference more, and whose members, __repr__, transforms and saves read the object of its id in the ML cache, of no training summary: of the session that is active when it is made, as the members of this client's models are of their model's session (see the native ML section below), where PySpark's _call_java reads the active session of each call. Of a model made on a thread of no active session, they read the session of each call, as PySpark's: a member and a save of the active session, which raise PySpark's bare AssertionError of none, and a transform of the dataset's. So does a model whose _java_obj is assigned a RemoteModelRef, as of the super().__init__(java_model) of a Python subclass of an __init__ of its own, whose JavaWrapper.__init__ sets it, and also a model of another server-side object (of a fit, of another reference), which gives its object up, as PySpark's model is of its _java_obj: it no longer releases the reference before, nor deletes the object of its fit, which stays in the ML cache until the session ends, as PySpark's __del__ releases a reference of its _java_obj only. A model whose _java_obj is assigned None (or another value) is of no object (see "A model of no server-side object" below), and one whose _java_obj is deleted raises PySpark's AttributeError of the attribute at a read of it, of its members and in its __del__, of no release. The model keeps its object in the ML cache as PySpark's: the __del__ of the model releases a reference of the RemoteModelRef (release_ref) at each call, when it is collected and at an explicit call, as PySpark's, of the model alone, and else the last of its transforms, which keep the object as PySpark's transforms keep their model (__source_transformer__), releases one when it is gone, if no __del__ did (an explicit __del__ of a model whose transform lives releases none, where PySpark's releases one at each call); the release deletes the object at the last reference, through the active session. An error of the release (an AssertionError of a reference released too often) is unraisable in the __del__ of the model's class (JavaWrapper.__del__), as PySpark's. A model of a reference in a reference cycle (ref.owner = model) is collected, and its __del__ releases the reference, as PySpark's. Params.copy adds a reference (add_ref), which the copy releases, and copy.copy adds none and releases one too, as PySpark's copies do, so the collection of a shallow copy of a model of a reference of one deletes the object of the original. Another java_model raises PySpark's bare AssertionError (of its assert isinstance(java_model, RemoteModelRef)).

    tests/golden/ml_api.json holds the API of every public class of pyspark.ml, and python/tests/test_ml_api_offline.py compares each native class with it: the members, the kinds of the members, and the parameters of each method and keyword-only constructor. tests/golden/misc_api_cases.json holds the same for NaiveBayes, MultilayerPerceptronClassifier, FMClassifier and OneVsRest, from python/tests/_misc_api_cases.py and scripts/capture_misc_api_cases_golden.py, and test_misc_api_offline.py and test_misc_api_e2e.py compare them. tests/golden/ml_api_cases.json holds the results of PySpark 4.3.0 of the cases of python/tests/_ml_api_cases.py (the Params API, the models, summaries, transforms and persistence), which test_ml_api_offline.py and test_ml_api_e2e.py compare with. tests/golden/ml_unbound_models.json holds the models of no server-side object of every model class of PySpark 4.3.0 of JavaModel(java_model=None) (python/tests/_ml_unbound_cases.py, scripts/capture_ml_unbound_models_golden.py), which test_ml_api_e2e.py compares with.

    The tree classes have their own cases, python/tests/_tree_api_cases.py and tests/golden/tree_api_cases.json, with test_tree_api_offline.py and test_tree_api_e2e.py.

    Native regression models (Rust API)¶

    Each regression estimator of spark_connect::ml fits its typed model, as PySpark's fit returns one: LinearRegression::fit returns a LinearRegressionModel (the fit of the Estimator trait boxes it). A member of a model is a fetch of its server-side object, as _call_java of PySpark 4.3.0 reads the member, and returns the type that PySpark returns:

    • LinearRegressionModel and GeneralizedLinearRegressionModel: coefficients, intercept (and scale of a linear regression), evaluate(&dataset), and the training summary of a fit, has_summary() and summary(). As PySpark's fit over Spark Connect, the fit sets up the training summary client-side, of the training dataset, so that the server can recreate a lost summary. A loaded model has no training summary.
    • Their summaries implement LinearRegressionSummaryApi (and TrainingSummary of the training summary) and GeneralizedLinearRegressionSummaryApi, whose training summary also has its solver, its iterations, the statistics of the coefficients and describe(). A DataFrame member, such as predictions or residuals(residuals_type), is an attribute relation of the summary. The summary of evaluate is its own server-side object, which the client does not release, as in PySpark.
    • AFTSurvivalRegressionModel (coefficients, intercept, scale, predict_quantiles), IsotonicRegressionModel (boundaries, predictions, num_features, and predict of a single feature value) and FMRegressionModel (intercept, linear, factors).
    • DecisionTreeRegressionModel implements DecisionTreeModel, and RandomForestRegressionModel and GBTRegressionModel implement TreeEnsembleModel, of trees that are each a DecisionTreeRegressionModel of its own server-side object. GBTRegressionModel has evaluate_each_iteration(&dataset, loss).
    • Every model but the isotonic one implements PredictionModel (num_features, predict), and every estimator and model has load(&session, path), as PySpark's load, besides write().

    The values are the server's, and the members are those that the server allows of each model (MLUtils of Spark 4.3.0). The native Python classes of pyspark.ml.connect.regression have them too, with PySpark's names: their fit returns the typed model.

    Native clustering models (Rust API)¶

    Each clustering estimator of spark_connect::ml fits its typed model, as the regression ones do (see "Native regression models (Rust API)"): KMeans::fit returns a KMeansModel.

    • KMeansModel and BisectingKMeansModel: cluster_centers() (the rows of the server's clusterCenterMatrix, as PySpark reads them), num_features, predict of a cluster index, compute_cost(&dataset) of a bisecting model (which PySpark deprecates since 3.0.0, in favor of ClusteringEvaluator and of the training summary's cost), and the training summary of a fit.
    • GaussianMixtureModel: weights, gaussians_df(), gaussians() (a MultivariateGaussian of each collected row of gaussiansDF, as PySpark builds them client-side), predict, predict_probability and the training summary of a fit.
    • The training summaries implement ClusteringSummary: KMeansSummary and BisectingKMeansSummary (training_cost), and GaussianMixtureSummary (probability_col, probability, log_likelihood).
    • LDAModel, LocalLDAModel and DistributedLDAModel implement LDAModelApi (describe_topics, topics_matrix, log_likelihood, log_perplexity, ...). As PySpark's LDA._create_model, a fit is local or distributed by its optimizer, and LDAModel::into_local() or into_distributed() gives the model of its class. A distributed model has to_local(), training_log_likelihood, log_prior and get_checkpoint_files.
    • Every estimator and model has load(&session, path). PowerIterationClustering has the uid of the algorithm, and write() and load save and load it as PySpark does: the params of the server's PowerIterationClusteringWrapper.

    The native Python classes of pyspark.ml.connect.clustering have them too, with PySpark's names: their fit returns the typed model, and LDAModel is the base class of the two LDA models, as PySpark's.

    Native LogisticRegression¶

    The native spark_connect::ml::LogisticRegression fits Spark's JVM org.apache.spark.ml.classification.LogisticRegression through the Connect ML protocol. Its training features must be ML vectors, in the Rust API as in Python; convert an array column with array_to_vector before fitting. The Python class, pyspark.ml.connect.classification.LogisticRegression, follows pyspark.ml.classification.LogisticRegression, PySpark's standard API over Spark Connect, not the deprecated PyTorch class of pyspark.ml.connect. Its keyword-only constructor takes the 21 params of pyspark.ml's, with its defaults and TypeConverters: featuresCol, labelCol, predictionCol, maxIter, regParam, elasticNetParam, tol, fitIntercept, threshold, thresholds, probabilityCol, rawPredictionCol, standardization, weightCol, aggregationDepth, family, the matrices lowerBoundsOnCoefficients and upperBoundsOnCoefficients, the vectors lowerBoundsOnIntercepts and upperBoundsOnIntercepts, and maxBlockSizeInMB. It has a setter of each, and setParams. A fit sends a matrix or a vector as PySpark's ML serializer sends a DenseMatrix, a SparseMatrix or a vector; in the Rust API, Params::set_param_matrix sends an MlMatrix so. The params of the PyTorch training of the deprecated class (numTrainWorkers, batchSize, learningRate, momentum and seed) raise the TypeError of an unexpected keyword argument. As pyspark.ml's, the constructor and setParams raise PySpark's ValueError when the threshold and the thresholds that are set disagree (setParams leaves the params that it is given set), and setThreshold and setThresholds leave the other one set, as they do over Spark Connect, where the clear of pyspark.ml does nothing: a fit then sends both, and the JVM raises its error when they disagree. Its model outputs the columns of the JVM model, as pyspark.ml's does: a rawPrediction and a probability vector, and a double prediction. The JVM checks the labels (an integer in [0, 100), not null), the types of the columns, and the output columns that the DataFrame has already, and raises its errors, as with pyspark.ml: the client makes no checks of its own, and sends no query of the labels.

    Fitting returns a server-cached model. Prediction uses that model's object reference and must use the same Spark session. Models are obtained through Estimator::fit; direct struct construction of LogisticRegressionModel is no longer supported. The cached model stays available while the model, a clone of it, or a DataFrame that it transformed is alive, including when such a DataFrame is executed later. When the last of them is dropped, the cached model is deleted on the server, as pyspark.ml does, without waiting for the server; a fit that is dropped at once leaves nothing cached. A Python model of a RemoteModelRef that a program gives its constructor releases a reference of the RemoteModelRef instead, as PySpark's model does, which deletes the object at the last (see "A model of a RemoteModelRef" above).

    The Rust transform of a fitted model rejects a DataFrame of another session. In Python, as with PySpark, it returns a DataFrame whose analysis or execution fails on the server of that session with CONNECT_ML.CACHE_INVALID.

    In Python, a fit, its training summary and the members of its model (predict, __repr__, the summary's DataFrames) are sent over the session of the dataset and of the model, so ML works on a session that is not the active one (a second session of SparkSession.builder.remote(...).create()) and on a thread of no active session. This is a deliberate difference from PySpark, which sends the members of a model and of a summary (_call_java, the DataFrame methods of try_remote_attribute_relation) over SparkSession.getActiveSession(), and its fits of the dataset's session (dataset.sparkSession):

    • On a session that is not the active one, PySpark's members of a model and of its summary, the summary's DataFrames and the model's __repr__ fail with CONNECT_ML.CACHE_INVALID (Cannot retrieve Summary object ... from the ML cache), and so do the fits that read a member of their model: of LogisticRegression and RandomForestClassifier, whose _summaryCls reads numClasses, and of GBTClassifier, whose params walk of _resetUid reads its trees. The fits of the others (LinearRegression, DecisionTreeClassifier, KMeans, StringIndexer, ...) and the transforms succeed.
    • On a thread of no active session (a thread that the program starts, as of fitMultiple shared by threads), the same members and fits raise PySpark's bare AssertionError (its assert session is not None), where this client's run.
    • PySpark deletes a dropped model through the active session (del_remote_cache): a model of a session that is not the active one is not deleted from the ML cache of its session, and one dropped on a thread of no active session neither. This client deletes it in its own session (see "The removal of the relation of a dropped checkpoint, and the deletion of a dropped model" in dev/parity/known-differences.md).

    A model used from a session other than its own fails on both.

    The native estimators, transformers and evaluators of pyspark.ml.connect take keyword-only constructor parameters. The evaluators follow standard pyspark.ml.evaluation: multiclass classification defaults to metricName="f1", and binary classification uses rawPredictionCol="rawPrediction". Each value is converted by the TypeConverters of its param in PySpark (an integral float for an int param, for example), and a value that the converter rejects raises its TypeError. A fit, transform or evaluate sends only the params that the constructor or a setter set, so the server applies its defaults to the others, as with PySpark. An int beyond a long raises PySpark's VALUE_NOT_BETWEEN at the next fit, transform or save. A list of ints (layers, indices, numBucketsArray) is sent as PySpark's literal of it, of the type of its first element (an integer, else a long) of every element: a list of an element out of the range of that type raises PySpark's bare AssertionError at the next fit, transform or save, and one whose first element is beyond a long VALUE_NOT_BETWEEN. Of several such params, the error is of the first of PySpark's _paramMap. None is sent as a null literal, as PySpark sends it: the server reads a null bootstrap as false and rejects the other nulls. A param that is not given is not sent, so the server's default applies. A list param without elements is accepted by its constructor and setters, and a fit, transform or evaluate raises PySpark's CANNOT_INFER_ARRAY_ELEMENT_TYPE for it, as PySpark's serialization does.

    The getThreshold, getThresholds, setThreshold and setThresholds methods of the estimator and of its model, and their threshold and thresholds Param objects, are PySpark's _LogisticRegressionParams methods, in Python, so they match its errors: the ValueError of the equivalent threshold when thresholds has a length other than two, and of an inconsistent pair, the ZeroDivisionError and TypeError of their arithmetic, and the KeyError of an unset thresholds. The %g values of the inconsistency message are formatted by Python.

    A native class has the setParams of its pyspark.ml class. It is keyword-only, takes the params of the constructor, sets the params that it is given, in order, and returns the object, as PySpark's _set does. As PySpark's, the setParams of LogisticRegression then checks its threshold and thresholds, whichever call set them, as its constructor does. Its signature is PySpark's for every class, the feature stages and the validators included. The params argument of a fit, transform or evaluate, which pyspark.ml takes as a param map, is accepted: None and an empty dict leave the params as they are. A non-empty map is copied with the map, as Estimator.fit and Transformer.transform copy it, so a key that is not a Param raises PySpark's TypeError of that copy. A list or tuple of maps to a fit is fitted as pyspark.ml fits it: one model per map, in order, returned as a list, so that [] fits nothing and does not read the dataset. Any other value raises the TypeError that pyspark.ml raises for it. The params argument is checked first, before the dataset, as pyspark.ml checks it.

    Native fit, transform and evaluate require a Spark DataFrame, matching the dataset contract of standard pyspark.ml over Spark Connect. Passing a pandas DataFrame raises the AttributeError of its own sparkSession ('DataFrame' object has no attribute 'sparkSession'), as pyspark.ml does, before the params of the class are validated, the schema is read or any request is sent (the helper calls below raise PySpark's error of their serialization). Only a params argument that is not a param map is checked before it (see above). This also applies to empty pandas DataFrames. The evaluate of a model's summary (LogisticRegressionModel.evaluate and its peers) raises the TypeError that pyspark.ml raises for a dataset that is not a DataFrame. The native Pipeline and validators raise the errors of PySpark's: a Pipeline that of its first stage, and a validator the AttributeError of the select of its split. A OneVsRest fits a dataset that is not a Spark DataFrame by PySpark's own OneVsRest._fit, so a pandas DataFrame raises the KeyError that it raises (see Native OneVsRest). Convert local data explicitly with df = spark.createDataFrame(pdf) before calling estimator.fit(df), model.transform(df) or evaluator.evaluate(df). Transforms return Spark DataFrames; call toPandas() explicitly when a local result is needed. The deprecated pyspark.ml.connect pandas contract is not the target for these native methods. The feature stages described below use the same Spark DataFrame contract.

    Another object than a DataFrame raises the AttributeError of sparkSession, the attribute that pyspark.ml reads of the dataset first ('int' object has no attribute 'sparkSession' of fit(5)), before any request. Of an object that has a sparkSession, a fit, a transform and an evaluate read the client of the session (a transform asserts a session that is not None first, of PySpark's bare AssertionError) and then the _plan of the object, of their AttributeErrors ('HasSession' object has no attribute '_plan'), as PySpark's try_remote_fit, try_remote_transform_relation and try_remote_evaluate do, and an object of both is the DataFrame of its plan and session (DataFrame(plan, session)), whose relation they send, as PySpark sends that of the plan. A transform reads them before it asserts the server-side object of a model and serializes its params, which PySpark does before it reads the _plan. The methods that pyspark.ml runs as a helper or attribute call read nothing of their dataset. PowerIterationClustering.assignClusters, PrefixSpan.findFrequentSequentialPatterns, and ALSModel.recommendForUserSubset and recommendForItemSubset, send a dataset that is not a DataFrame as PySpark does, and the server rejects it when the result is analyzed or collected. PySpark's serialization (pyspark.ml.connect.serialize) converts any other value to a literal, as LiteralExpression._from_value does: - One that cannot be a literal raises PySpark's error of the conversion. That includes a pandas DataFrame, which raises UNSUPPORTED_DATA_TYPE here rather than the AttributeError above, as with pyspark.ml, and an empty list, which raises CANNOT_INFER_ARRAY_ELEMENT_TYPE. - A (value, DataType) pair is the literal of that type, as LiteralExpression(value, dataType) makes it, whose asserts raise a bare AssertionError of a value that is not of the type (("x", IntegerType())) and of a type that no literal has (a MapType, StructType, CharType, VarcharType, YearMonthIntervalType or UDT), also with a None value, as with pyspark.ml (tests/golden/ml_fetch_pairs.json records the pairs of official PySpark). - PySpark sends the others for the server to reject: an ML vector or matrix as they are, and a (value, DataType) pair as a literal of that type. assignClusters, PrefixSpan's findFrequentSequentialPatterns and ALSModel's recommendForUserSubset and recommendForItemSubset send them too, in a lazy DataFrame that the server rejects when it is analyzed or collected.

    As with pyspark.ml, a fit sends the DataFrame as it is, and the transform of a model is the transform of the DataFrame as it is, which sends no request: the JVM estimators and models check the columns, and raise their errors of a column of a wrong type, or of an output column that the DataFrame has already, in the fit, or when the transformed DataFrame is analyzed or run.

    The column params of a fit and of a transform are sent as they are given, as pyspark.ml sends them, and the JVM reads a column by its exact name: featuresCol="Features" of a column features raises the JVM's FIELD_NOT_FOUND in a fit, as with pyspark.ml, while some JVM models resolve such a name. Evaluators send column names and types unchanged too, leaving their validation to Spark as standard pyspark.ml.evaluation does. Their metric results are Spark's double-precision values.

    The native estimators take the VectorUDT features of pyspark.ml: the JVM raises its error of an array featuresCol (Column features must be of type class org.apache.spark.ml.linalg.VectorUDT:... but was actually class org.apache.spark.sql.types.ArrayType:array<double>.), in the fit and when the transformed DataFrame is analyzed. Convert an array column with pyspark.ml.functions.array_to_vector first, as for pyspark.ml. (The JVM KMeans, BisectingKMeans, GaussianMixture and LDA also take arrays whose elements cannot be null, which the arrays of createDataFrame can be.)

    The feature stages, including StandardScaler and MaxAbsScaler, use Spark's JVM implementations through Connect. Vector-valued inputs must use VectorUDT; convert numeric arrays explicitly with pyspark.ml.functions.array_to_vector. StandardScaler defaults to withMean=False, withStd=True, matching standard pyspark.ml.

    The Rust LogisticRegression and LogisticRegressionModel have the params of pyspark.ml, with one generated getter and setter per param (set_max_iter and max_iter(), set_features_col and features_col(), and so on), and PARAM_NAMES lists them. LogisticRegression::feature_col and set_feature_col, the names of v4.2.0, stay as deprecated aliases of features_col and set_features_col. threshold(), thresholds(), set_threshold and set_thresholds follow _LogisticRegressionParams: the getters check that the two agree to 1e-5 and threshold() requires two thresholds. The setters leave the other param set, as over Spark Connect, where the clear of pyspark.ml does nothing, so a disagreement raises the getters' error. Their arithmetic is PySpark's, in Python: a zero divisor of the equivalent threshold of two thresholds, 1 / (1 + t0 / t1) (a zero t1, or a ratio of -1), is the error "float division by zero" of its ZeroDivisionError, of threshold() and, with a threshold set too, of thresholds(). The model has the typed members of LinearSVCModel (see "Native LinearSVC" below), plus coefficient_matrix() and intercept_vector(); coefficients() and intercept() raise the server's error of a multinomial model, as PySpark does. The model's summary() is binary for at most two classes, of the numClasses that it fetches when called (PySpark fetches it at the fit), and evaluate makes the same choice on its dataset. has_summary() is client side. The Rust API differs from pyspark.ml as the LinearSVC section says.

    Native RandomForestRegressor¶

    spark_connect::ml::RandomForestRegressor fits Spark's JVM random forest regressor through Connect using ML vector features and numeric labels. Rust builder setters and getters cover all 19 PySpark constructor parameters, including instance weights, bootstrap sampling, feature sampling and optional per-tree leaf indices. Unset parameters use Spark's defaults; a seed can select another reproducible sample.

    The native Python estimator is available from pyspark.ml.connect.regression.RandomForestRegressor and pyspark.ml.connect.RandomForestRegressor. It uses PySpark's keyword-only constructor names, defaults and parameter conversions, with mutating setters. Fitted models expose DataFrame transform for the fitting session. Models, clones and lazy transformed DataFrames retain the cached server model until their last owner drops. The Rust model's members and persistence are in "Native regression models (Rust API)". In Python, RandomForestRegressor and its RandomForestRegressionModel follow the full API of pyspark.ml (see "Native ML classes of the full pyspark.ml API"): the Params API, trees (each a DecisionTreeRegressionModel, fetched at the fit and at a load, as PySpark's), treeWeights, getNumTrees, totalNumNodes, featureImportances, toDebugString, numFeatures, predict and predictLeaf of a vector, and save and load.

    Native GBTRegressor¶

    spark_connect::ml::GBTRegressor fits Spark's JVM gradient-boosted regressor through Connect with ML vector features and continuous numeric labels. Rust setters/getters cover all 22 PySpark constructor parameters. It supports squared and absolute loss, instance weights, row/feature sampling, learning rate, iteration limits and validation-set early stopping.

    The Python binding is available as pyspark.ml.connect.regression.GBTRegressor and pyspark.ml.connect.GBTRegressor. Its keyword-only signature and 21 named setters match PySpark. validationTol has no Python setter. The reference's signature advertises validationTol=0.1, but omitting it leaves the parameter unset and uses Spark's 0.01 default. This binding preserves that behavior; GBTRegressor(validationTol=0.1) explicitly sends 0.1. The Rust getter returns 0.01 when unset.

    maxIter limits boosting iterations, and stepSize scales each tree after the first. With validationIndicatorCol, false rows train and true rows validate; early stopping can fit fewer trees. DataFrame transforms expose predictions and an optional vector of leaf IDs, one per fitted tree. Models, clones and lazy transformed DataFrames retain the cached model in its fitting session until the last owner is dropped. The Rust model's members and persistence are in "Native regression models (Rust API)". In Python, GBTRegressor and its GBTRegressionModel follow the full API of pyspark.ml, as the random forest ones do: the Params API, the members of a tree ensemble, featureImportances, evaluateEachIteration(dataset, loss), and save and load.

    Native GBTClassifier¶

    spark_connect::ml::GBTClassifier fits Spark's JVM gradient-boosted binary classifier through Connect with ML vector features and labels 0 or 1. Rust builder setters/getters cover all 22 PySpark constructor parameters, plus probability/raw prediction column names and class thresholds. Unset parameters use Spark's defaults. A seed controls split-candidate row sampling, subsampling and feature sampling.

    The Python binding is available as pyspark.ml.connect.classification.GBTClassifier and pyspark.ml.connect.GBTClassifier. Its keyword-only constructor mirrors PySpark. Probability/raw prediction column names and thresholds are set through setProbabilityCol, setRawPredictionCol and setThresholds. validationTol is a constructor argument; PySpark does not provide a Python setter for it.

    maxIter limits boosting iterations, and stepSize scales each tree after the first. With validationIndicatorCol, false rows train the trees and true rows validate early stopping; the resulting ensemble can contain fewer than maxIter trees. DataFrame transforms expose predictions, probabilities, raw margins and an optional vector of leaf IDs, one per fitted tree. Instance weights are supported. Models, clones and lazy transformed DataFrames retain the cached model in its fitting session until the last owner is dropped. The Rust model has the typed accessors of PySpark's GBTClassificationModel: trees (regression tree models), tree_weights, get_num_trees, total_num_nodes, feature_importances, to_debug_string, predict, predict_leaf, predict_raw, predict_probability, evaluate_each_iteration(&df) and the supported-value lists. It has load, and write().save(path). Unlike PySpark, the fit does not fetch the trees up front; they are fetched when asked. In Python, GBTClassifier and its GBTClassificationModel follow the full API of pyspark.ml (see "Native ML classes of the full pyspark.ml API"); as PySpark's, its fit reads the trees right after the Fit.

    Native DecisionTreeRegressor¶

    spark_connect::ml::DecisionTreeRegressor fits Spark's JVM decision tree regressor through Connect with ML vector features and continuous numeric labels. Rust builder setters/getters cover all 16 PySpark constructor parameters, including set_variance_col, set_weight_col and set_leaf_col. Unset parameters use Spark's defaults.

    The native Python binding is available as pyspark.ml.connect.regression.DecisionTreeRegressor and pyspark.ml.connect.DecisionTreeRegressor. Its keyword-only constructor mirrors PySpark's parameter names and defaults. Mutating setFeaturesCol, setMaxDepth, setVarianceCol, etc. methods return the estimator.

    Fitted models expose DataFrame transform, including predictions, optional weighted label variance in the predicted leaf and optional scalar double leaf indices. Variance is computed within the predicted leaf; it is not a confidence interval for the prediction. Models, clones and lazy transformed DataFrames keep the cached server model alive in its fitting session until the last owner is dropped. The Rust model's members and persistence are in "Native regression models (Rust API)". In Python, DecisionTreeRegressor and its DecisionTreeRegressionModel follow the full API of pyspark.ml (see "Native ML classes of the full pyspark.ml API"): the Params API, depth, numNodes, featureImportances, toDebugString, numFeatures, predict and predictLeaf of a vector, and save and load.

    Native DecisionTreeClassifier¶

    spark_connect::ml::DecisionTreeClassifier trains Spark's JVM decision tree classifier through Connect with ML vector features and non-negative integer class labels below numClasses. Rust builder setters cover all 18 PySpark constructor parameters, including pruneTree (Spark 4.3.0), plus class thresholds. Unset parameters use Spark's defaults.

    The Python binding is available as pyspark.ml.connect.classification.DecisionTreeClassifier and pyspark.ml.connect.DecisionTreeClassifier. Its keyword-only constructor matches PySpark's parameter names and defaults; mutating setFeaturesCol, setMaxDepth, etc. methods and setThresholds return the estimator.

    Fitted models expose DataFrame transform, including prediction, probability, raw per-leaf weighted class counts and an optional scalar double leaf index. Models, clones and lazy transformed DataFrames retain the cached model in its fitting session until the last owner is dropped. The Rust model has the typed accessors of PySpark's DecisionTreeClassificationModel: depth, num_nodes, feature_importances, to_debug_string, predict, predict_leaf, predict_raw, predict_probability and supported_impurities, and load; write().save(path) saves it. In Python, DecisionTreeClassifier and its DecisionTreeClassificationModel follow the full API of pyspark.ml (see "Native ML classes of the full pyspark.ml API").

    Native RandomForestClassifier¶

    spark_connect::ml::RandomForestClassifier trains Spark's JVM random forest classifier through Connect with ML vector features and non-negative integer class labels below numClasses. Rust builder setters cover all 22 PySpark constructor parameters, including pruneTree (Spark 4.3.0), plus class thresholds. Unset parameters use Spark's defaults. Without a seed, the sampling uses Spark's default seed (the hash of the class name), as with PySpark, so fits are reproducible either way.

    The native Python binding is available as pyspark.ml.connect.classification.RandomForestClassifier and pyspark.ml.connect.RandomForestClassifier. Its keyword-only constructor matches PySpark's parameter names and defaults, with mutating setFeaturesCol, setNumTrees, etc. methods and setThresholds.

    Fitted models expose DataFrame transform, including prediction, probability, raw prediction and optional per-tree leaf index columns. Models, clones and lazy transformed DataFrames retain the cached model in its fitting session until the last owner is dropped. The Rust model has the typed accessors of PySpark's RandomForestClassificationModel: trees (decision tree models), tree_weights, get_num_trees, total_num_nodes, feature_importances, to_debug_string, predict, predict_leaf, predict_raw, predict_probability, has_summary, summary() (the binary or multiclass training summary, chosen by numClasses), evaluate(&df) (the binary or multiclass summary of a dataset), the supported-value lists, and load; write().save(path) saves it. A loaded model has no training summary. The fit does not fetch numClasses and the trees up front as PySpark does; summary() and trees() fetch them when asked. In Python, RandomForestClassifier, its RandomForestClassificationModel and the four random forest summaries follow the full API of pyspark.ml (see "Native ML classes of the full pyspark.ml API"); as PySpark's, its fit reads numClasses and then the trees right after the Fit.

    Native KMeans¶

    spark_connect::ml::KMeans fits Spark's JVM K-means estimator with ML vector features through the Connect ML protocol. Rust builder setters cover all 12 constructor parameters, including set_k, set_seed, set_weight_col, set_distance_measure and set_solver. Unset parameters use Spark's defaults. Without a seed, the initialization uses Spark's default seed (the hash of the class name), as with PySpark, so fits are reproducible either way. Cluster numbers are arbitrary: compare membership rather than relying on particular numeric labels.

    The native Python binding is available as pyspark.ml.connect.clustering.KMeans and pyspark.ml.connect.KMeans, with the upstream keyword-only constructor and mutating parameter setters. Fitted models expose DataFrame transform in the fitting session. Models, clones and transformed DataFrames keep the cached server model alive until the last owner is dropped. The Rust model's members, summaries and persistence are in "Native clustering models (Rust API)". In Python, KMeans, its KMeansModel and KMeansSummary follow the full API of pyspark.ml (see "Native ML classes of the full pyspark.ml API"): the Params API, clusterCenters(), numFeatures, predict of a vector, the training summary (k, clusterSizes, trainingCost, numIter, predictions and cluster, of the transform of the training dataset, as PySpark's), and save and load. The vendored pyspark.ml.clustering API is unchanged.

    Native LinearRegression¶

    spark_connect::ml::LinearRegression fits Spark's JVM linear regression estimator through the Connect ML protocol, with ML vector features and numeric labels. Rust builder setters cover all 15 constructor parameters, including regularization, instance weights, solver selection and squared-error or Huber loss. Unset parameters use Spark's defaults.

    The native Python binding is available as pyspark.ml.connect.regression.LinearRegression and pyspark.ml.connect.LinearRegression. Its keyword-only constructor matches the JVM PySpark estimator's parameter names and defaults, with mutating setFeaturesCol, setRegParam, etc. methods. Fitted models expose transform on DataFrames from the fitting session. The model, its clones and transformed DataFrames keep the cached server model alive until the last owner is dropped. The Rust model's members and persistence are in "Native regression models (Rust API)". In Python, LinearRegression, its LinearRegressionModel, LinearRegressionSummary and LinearRegressionTrainingSummary follow the full API of pyspark.ml (see "Native ML classes of the full pyspark.ml API"): the Params API, coefficients, intercept, scale, numFeatures, predict of a vector, the training summary (of the training dataset, as PySpark's), evaluate, and save and load.

    Native LinearSVC¶

    spark_connect::ml::LinearSVC trains Spark's JVM binary linear support vector classifier through the Connect ML protocol. Use vector features and labels 0 or 1. Rust builder setters cover all 13 constructor parameters, including set_reg_param, set_tol, set_weight_col and set_threshold. Unset parameters use Spark's defaults. The Python binding is available as pyspark.ml.connect.classification.LinearSVC and pyspark.ml.connect.LinearSVC, with the upstream keyword-only constructor parameters and mutating setFeaturesCol, setRegParam, etc. methods.

    fit returns a server-cached model; transform must use the fitting session. Clones and lazy prediction DataFrames keep the cached model usable while they are alive. When the last of the model, its clones and the DataFrames it transformed is dropped, the server deletes the cached model, as pyspark.ml does. In Python, LinearSVC, its LinearSVCModel and their summaries follow the full API of pyspark.ml (see "Native ML classes of the full pyspark.ml API"): the Params API, coefficients, intercept, numClasses, numFeatures, predict and predictRaw of a vector, summary(), evaluate, and save and load.

    The Rust LinearSVCModel has the typed members of pyspark.ml: coefficients(), intercept(), num_classes(), num_features(), predict(&vector) and predict_raw(&vector), has_summary() (client side), summary() (the training summary, with accuracy(), area_under_roc(), objective_history(), total_iterations() and the curve DataFrames roc() and pr()), evaluate(&df) (the same summary members on a dataset), LinearSVCModel::load(&session, path), and model.write().overwrite().save(path). Its setters set_features_col, set_prediction_col, set_raw_prediction_col and set_threshold set the params of its transform. LinearSVC::fit_multiple(&df, &maps) fits one model per param map, and LinearSVC::load loads an estimator. Differences from pyspark.ml: summary() is a typed value rather than a Python object, fit_multiple returns a Vec rather than an iterator, and read() is load, with no reader object. The summary's DataFrames are built on the training DataFrame, as PySpark builds them.

    In Python, LogisticRegression and its model follow the full API of pyspark.ml in the same way, with coefficientMatrix, interceptVector, predictProbability, the summary property and hasSummary.
